CONFIDENTIALITY AND INVESTIGATOR STATEMENT 
The information contained in this protocol and all other information relevant to TYRA-300 are 
the confidential and proprietary information of Tyra Biosciences, Inc. and, except as may be 
required by federal, state, or local laws or regulation, may not be disclosed to others without 
prior written permission of Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
I have read the protocol, including all appendices, and I agree that it contains all of the necessary 
information for me and my staff to conduct this study as described.  I will conduct this study as 
outlined herein, in accordance with applicable national, state, and local regulations and will make 
a reasonable effort to complete the study within the time designated. 
I will provide all study personnel under my supervision copies of the protocol and any 
amendments, and access to all information provided by Tyra Biosciences, Inc. or specified 
designees.  I will discuss the material with them to ensure that they are fully informed about Tyra 
Biosciences, Inc. and the study.

STUDY SYNOPSIS 
Protocol Title: 
A Multicenter, Open-label Phase 1/2 Study of TYRA-300 in 
Advanced Urothelial Carcinoma and Other Solid Tumors with 
Activating FGFR3 Gene Alterations (SURF-301) 
Study Number: 
TYR300-101 
Clinical Phase: 
Phase 1/2 
Investigator(s)/Study 
Center(s): 
This study will be conducted at approximately 60 study centers, with 
approximately 60 investigators, located in Australia, Canada, the 
Czech Republic, France, Germany, Italy, the Netherlands, South 
Korea, Spain, Taiwan, the United Kingdom, and the United States.  
 
, a contract research organization, will oversee operational 
aspects of this study on behalf of Tyra Biosciences, Inc., the sponsor 
of the study. 
Objectives: 
Primary Objectives: 
• To determine the optimal and maximum tolerated doses 
(MTD), and the recommended Phase 2 dose (RP2D) of 
TYRA-300 in participants with advanced solid tumors 
(Phase 1, Parts A and B). 
• To evaluate the preliminary antitumor activity of TYRA-300 
at the RP2D in participants in selected tumor expansion 
cohorts with activating fibroblast growth factor receptor 
(FGFR) 3 gene alterations (Phase 2). 
Secondary Objectives: 
• To characterize the safety and tolerability of TYRA-300 in 
participants with advanced cancer (Phase 1, Parts A and B, 
and Phase 2). 
• To conduct a preliminary characterization of the 
pharmacokinetics (PK) and pharmacodynamics in participants 
treated with TYRA-300 (Phase 1, Parts A and B, and 
Phase 2). 
• To characterize objective response rate (ORR) in participants 
with activating FGFR3 gene alterations (Phase 1, Part B). 
• To characterize duration of response (DOR; Phase 1, Part B 
and Phase 2). 
CCI

===PAGE 5===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 5 of 167 
• To characterize disease control rate (DCR) >12 weeks 
(Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2). 
• To characterize time to response (TTR; Phase 1, Part B and 
Phase 2). 
• To characterize progression-free survival (PFS) in 
participants treated with TYRA-300 in specific tumor 
expansion cohorts (Cohorts 1 and 2 of Phase 2 only). 
Exploratory Objectives: 
• 
 
• 
 
 
 
• 
 
Study Design: 
This is a Phase 1/2, open-label, international, multicenter, dose 
escalation/dose expansion study of TYRA-300 that will be 
implemented in several parts: 
Phase 1, Part A: First-in-human cohorts with accelerated dose 
escalation. 
The study will use an interval 3 + 3 (i3 + 3) design with a target 
toxicity rate of the MTD of 0.3 (0.25 to 0.35).  Each dose will be 
assessed through the dose-limiting toxicity (DLT) evaluation period 
(Cycle 1 [28 days]) before the next dose level (DL) can be enrolled. 
DL1 and DL2 will proceed in an accelerated fashion: 1 participant 
with any advanced solid tumor who has exhausted all standard 
treatment options will be enrolled at the initial starting DL of 10 mg 
daily (DL1).  The study will escalate to the next DL (20 mg daily; 
DL2) if no DLT occurs during the DLT evaluation period.  The first 
dosing cohorts (DL1 and DL2) will utilize a stricter definition for 
DLT than DL3 and above: any ≥Grade 2 treatment-related adverse 
event (TRAE) will constitute a DLT for DL1 and DL2.  If either DL1 
or DL2 enrolls additional participants due to this stricter DLT 
definition, the study will convert to an i3 + 3 design and all 
subsequent dose escalation decisions will use the same DLT 
CCI
CCI
CCI

===PAGE 6===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 6 of 167 
definitions as for DL3 and above (ie, no longer use ≥Grade 2 TRAEs 
as the definition for DLTs). 
This process will be followed until the third DL (40 mg daily; DL3) 
is reached, when dose escalation will formally convert to an i3 + 3 
design and a minimum of 3 participants will be enrolled per DL.  
These cohorts may enroll 1 to 3 additional participants at a time if 
additional participants are required.  Dose escalation will continue up 
to DL6 (120 mg daily), until the MTD is determined, or until 
approximately 30 participants have been enrolled in Part A.  Doses in 
Part A above 120 mg daily will be limited to participants with 
advanced solid tumors with eligible FGFR3 alterations.  
The sponsor and study investigators will meet routinely at planned 
intervals to assess participant safety.  Doses will be increased no 
more than 50% of the previous dose beyond DL3, and intermediate 
doses may be explored.  The MTD will be the highest DL with no 
more than 35% of participants who have an adverse event (AE) that 
meets the criteria for a DLT. 
Within each DL in Phase 1, Part A, dosing of the first 2 participants 
will be staggered such that the second participant may not be dosed 
on the same day as the first participant. 
Phase 1, Part B: Starting at DL3, dose expansion cohorts will be 
opened to enroll participants with FGFR3 mutant tumors.  The 
purpose of the Part B cohorts is to study the pharmacodynamic and 
potential antitumor effects of TYRA-300 at several potentially 
pharmacologically active doses.  Each dose expansion cohort will 
open when the corresponding dose cohort in Part A meets the criteria 
for dose escalation.  Twice daily dosing (BID) cohorts may open as 
daily doses approach the MTD based on emerging PK data. 
Part B will enroll participants with locally advanced/metastatic solid 
tumors and an FGFR3 activating gene alteration (mutation or 
rearrangement) for whom an FGFR3 targeted therapy would be 
appropriate.  Participants with an eligible gene alteration, based on an 
authorized/approved validated local test, performed at a Clinical 
Laboratory Improvement Amendments (CLIA; or regional 
equivalent) certified laboratory, may be enrolled and a specimen 
must be submitted for testing using a sponsor-designated central 
assay.  Alternatively, in circumstances where tissue to perform a 
validated local test is not available, a central test, or a locally 
validated or approved assay may be used to identify eligible 
mutations in ctDNA in plasma.  Any number of prior therapies are 
allowed, including prior FGFR inhibitors.  Part B will also utilize the 
i3 + 3 design, but dose escalation will not apply.  Enrolment can 

===PAGE 7===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 7 of 167 
continue for up to 6 participants per cohort if the toxicity probability 
does not exceed 0.35 for the sum total of participants in Parts A and 
B at that DL. 
When doses approaching the MTD are identified in Part A, BID dose 
cohorts may be explored in Part B based on emerging PK data. Twice 
daily dosing cohorts will enroll participants with locally 
advanced/metastatic FGFR3 mutation positive urothelial carcinoma 
who have not received a prior FGFR inhibitor.  Twice daily dose 
cohorts will evaluate the PK of a tablet formulation that is to be used 
in the Phase 2 portion of the study.  Pharmacokinetics and AEs will 
be reviewed by the Safety Review Committee after 3 to 5 participants 
in new BID expansion cohorts to ensure there are no safety concerns 
before completing enrolment of the dose level. 
Note: Participants who are FGFR inhibitor-naïve should be 
counseled on the availability of approved therapies and the 
potential clinical benefit they are foregoing to enroll in this study. 
Note: Participants with metastatic urothelial carcinoma who have 
received a prior FGFR inhibitor and have an eligible FGFR3 
resistance mutation, or participants with other solid tumors with 
eligible FGFR3 alterations, are allowed with medical monitor 
approval. 
Intra-participant dose escalation will be allowed for participants in 
Part A and Part B cohorts under the following circumstances: 
• A higher dose has been tested in Part A and was deemed to be 
tolerated. 
• The participant has not had a >Grade 1 TRAE while taking 
TYRA-300. 
• If a ≥Grade 3 treatment-related toxicity in any participant 
occurs at a given DL, intra-participant dose escalation is not 
allowed at that DL. 
• In the opinion of the investigator, and with approval from the 
medical monitor, a higher dose under study in an enrolling or 
completed cohort may be safe and potentially more effective 
in treating the participant’s tumor than the current dose. 
• The participant has completed at least 2 cycles of study drug 
and has at least 1 postbaseline tumor imaging assessment.

Participants with evidence of progressive disease (PD), as defined by 
Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors version 1.1 
(RECIST v1.1), may continue to receive study drug if, in the opinion 
of the investigator and with approval from the medical monitor, the 
participant is deriving benefit from continuing TYRA-300.  The 
following criteria must be met in order to provide assurance that the 
participant is not exposed to an unreasonable risk: 
• Absence of symptoms and signs indicating clinically 
significant progression of disease. 
• No decline in Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group 
Performance Status (ECOG PS) from baseline. 
• Absence of symptomatic rapid disease progression requiring 
urgent medical intervention (eg, symptomatic pleural 
effusion, spinal cord compression, etc). 
• Participants who have experienced disease progression in the 
central nervous system (CNS) but have stable disease or 
better in the periphery, may continue to receive treatment 
with TYRA-300 after definitive treatment of the CNS lesions. 
At the time of radiographic progression, participants must also be 
reconsented and provided with details of all approved therapies, and 
potential clinical benefit, that the participant may be foregoing in 
order to continue receiving TYRA-300. 
Results from the dose escalation and dose expansion cohorts will be 
analyzed for safety, as well as potential relationships between dose, 
PK, 
, and preliminary antitumor 
activity of TYRA-300.  The RP2D will be selected using the totality 
of the Phase 1 data. 
Phase 2: Pivotal expansion cohorts in selected tumor populations at 
the RP2D. 
Participants will be enrolled in Phase 2 to further characterize 
preliminary efficacy, safety, and tolerability in the following cohorts: 
Cohort 1: Participants with locally advanced/metastatic urothelial 
carcinoma previously treated with an FGFR inhibitor who now have 
defined FGFR3 resistance mutations. 
• Participants with urothelial carcinoma will be screened and 
evaluated for eligible resistance mutations using a central test 
performed by a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) 
CCI

laboratory, or a locally validated or approved assay that 
assesses somatic mutations ctDNA from plasma.  Participants 
with an eligible gene alteration based on an 
authorized/approved local test, performed at a CLIA-certified 
(or regional equivalent) laboratory, may be enrolled and a 
specimen must be submitted for central testing using the 
sponsor-designated laboratory.  Confirmation is not necessary 
for these participants before starting TYRA-300. 
• For France only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have 
had prior approved therapies including platinum-based 
chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an 
antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the 
investigator the participant will not tolerate these therapies. 
• For Spain only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have had 
prior approved therapies including platinum-based 
chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an 
antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the 
investigator the participant will not tolerate any of these 
approved agents, or the participant refuses these therapies. 
Cohort 2: Participants with locally advanced/metastatic urothelial 
carcinoma with defined activating FGFR3 gene alterations who have 
not previously received an FGFR inhibitor. 
• Participants with urothelial carcinoma will be screened and 
evaluated for eligible activating mutations and 
rearrangements using a central test performed by a 
CLIA-certified laboratory (or regional equivalent), or a 
locally validated or approved assay, that assesses somatic 
mutations in ctDNA from plasma.  Participants with an 
eligible gene alteration based on an authorized/approved local 
test, performed at a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) 
laboratory, may be enrolled and a specimen must be 
submitted for central testing using the sponsor-designated 
laboratory.  Confirmation is not necessary for these 
participants before starting TYRA-300. 
• For France only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have 
had prior approved therapies including platinum-based 
chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an 
antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the 
investigator the participant will not tolerate these therapies. 
• For Spain only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have had 
prior approved therapies including platinum-based

chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an 
antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the 
investigator the participant will not tolerate any of these 
approved agents, or the participant refuses these therapies. 
Cohort 3: Participants with any locally advanced/metastatic solid 
tumor with defined activating FGFR3 gene alterations who have not 
previously received an FGFR inhibitor. 
• Participants will be enrolled based on the results of a Food 
and Drug Administration (FDA) authorized/approved 
companion diagnostic (CDx) or validated local test performed 
in a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory.  A 
blood specimen must be submitted to the central laboratory 
for analysis using the sponsor-designated laboratory.  
Confirmation is not necessary for these participants before 
starting TYRA-300. 
• With medical monitor approval, participants who progressed 
on a prior FGFR inhibitor may be enrolled if they have an 
activating FGFR3 alteration and a known FGFR3 acquired 
resistance mutation detected by central testing or other 
validated local test performed in a CLIA-certified (or regional 
equivalent) laboratory. 
• For France only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have 
received prior approved therapies unless, in the opinion of the 
investigator, the participant will not tolerate the approved 
therapies. 
• For Spain only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have 
received prior approved therapies unless, in the opinion of the 
investigator, the participant will not tolerate the approved 
agents, or the participant refuses these therapies. 
Participants in Phase 2 with evidence of PD, as defined by 
RECIST v1.1, may continue to receive study drug if they meet the 
same criteria as described for Phase 1. 
Duration of 
Treatment: 
All enrolled participants will receive TYRA-300 until disease 
progression, death, unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of participant’s 
consent for treatment, withdrawal from the study at the discretion of 
the investigator, or study termination, whichever occurs first. 
Planned Sample Size 
and Treatment 
Group(s): 
For Phase 1, the total number of participants enrolled will depend on 
the number of escalation cohorts and the number of dose expansion 
cohorts evaluated.  It is estimated that up to 80 participants could be 

===PAGE 11===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 11 of 167 
enrolled in Parts A and B combined (up to 30 participants in Part A 
and up to 50 participants in Part B). 
For Phase 2, approximately 110 participants will be enrolled in each 
of Cohorts 1 and 2.  A Simon’s 2-stage design will be used for 
enrollment.  In the first stage, 39 participants will be accrued and if 
there are 6 or fewer responses, enrollment to the cohort will be 
stopped for futility.  Otherwise, 71 additional participants will be 
accrued for a total of 110 participants.  The null hypothesis will be 
rejected if 23 or more responses are observed in 110 participants.  
This design yields a 1-sided type I error rate of 5% and a power of 
80% when the true response rate is 25%.  Responses are defined as 
complete response (CR) or partial response (PR) by RECIST v1.1 at 
any time while on therapy; for Cohort 1, clinical benefit (prolonged 
stable disease [SD] of ≥6 months while on therapy) will count as a 
response. 
Cohort 3 is exploratory, and approximately 40 participants will be 
enrolled in a single stage. 
Target Population: 
Phase 1, Part A: Participants with any advanced solid tumor who 
have exhausted all standard treatment options and have an FGFR 
gene alteration listed in Appendix 14.3.1 or 14.3.2. 
Phase 1, Part B: Participants with locally advanced/metastatic solid 
tumor and an FGFR3 activating gene alteration listed in 
Appendix 14.3.1 or 14.3.2 for whom an FGFR3 targeted therapy 
would be appropriate.  Expansion cohorts in Part B will dose 
TYRA-300 on a daily schedule.  When doses approaching the MTD 
are identified in Part A, BID dose cohorts may be explored in Part B 
based on emerging PK data.  The BID dosing cohorts will enroll 
participants with locally advanced/metastatic FGFR3 mutation 
positive urothelial carcinoma who have not received a prior FGFR 
inhibitor.  However, with medical monitor approval, participants with 
metastatic urothelial carcinoma who have received prior FGFR 
inhibitor and have an eligible FGFR3 resistance mutation, or 
participants with other solid tumors with eligible FGFR3 alterations, 
are allowed. 
Phase 2, Cohort 1: Participants with locally advanced/metastatic 
urothelial carcinoma previously treated with an FGFR inhibitor who 
now have defined FGFR3 resistance mutations. 
Phase 2, Cohort 2: Participants with locally advanced/metastatic 
urothelial carcinoma with defined activating FGFR3 gene alterations 
who have not previously received an FGFR inhibitor.

Phase 2, Cohort 3: Participants with any locally advanced/metastatic 
solid tumor with defined activating FGFR3 gene alterations who 
have not previously received an FGFR inhibitor. 
Note for France only: Participants with metastatic urothelial 
carcinoma should have previously received approved therapies, 
including platinum-based chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint 
inhibitor, and an antibody drug conjugate.  Participants with 
metastatic urothelial carcinoma may also be enrolled if they are not 
candidates for any of the approved therapies.  Participants with other 
tumor types (Cohort 3) should either have received approved 
standard therapies for their tumor type or not be candidates for such 
therapies. 
Note for Spain only: Participants with metastatic urothelial 
carcinoma should have previously received approved therapies, 
including platinum-based chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint 
inhibitor, and an antibody-drug conjugate.  Participants with 
metastatic urothelial carcinoma may also be enrolled if they are not 
candidates for any of the approved therapies or if they refuse these 
therapies.  Participants with other tumor types (Cohort 3) should 
either have received approved standard therapies for their tumor type, 
not be candidates for such therapies, or have refused these therapies. 
Eligibility Criteria: 
Inclusion Criteria for Phase 1, Part A: 
1. Male and female participants who are 18 years of age or older on 
the day of signing the Informed Consent Form (ICF). 
2. Life expectancy >12 weeks. 
3. Ability to understand and sign the ICF and comply with study 
procedures. 
4. ECOG PS ≤1. 
5. Participants with any histologically confirmed advanced solid 
tumor who have exhausted standard therapeutic options.  For 
doses of TYRA-300 above 120 mg QD or BID dose levels, 
participants must have an eligible FGFR3 gene mutation or 
fusion (see Appendix 14.3.1 and 14.3.2) diagnosed by any 
authorized/approved or validated local test performed in a 
CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory. 
6. Ability to swallow oral formulations. 
7. Disease evaluable by RECIST v1.1. 

===PAGE 13===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 13 of 167 
8. Adequate organ and bone marrow function as demonstrated by 
the following: 
a. Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) ≥1500/mm3. 
b. Platelet count ≥75,000/mm3. 
c. International normalized ratio (INR) ≤1.5 × upper limit of 
normal (ULN). 
i. Participants treated with anticoagulants (eg, warfarin or 
heparin) will be allowed to participate provided no prior 
evidence of an underlying abnormality in these parameters 
exists.  Close monitoring of at least weekly evaluations 
will be performed until INR is stable based on a predose 
measurement as defined by the local standard of care. 
d. Total bilirubin ≤1.5 × ULN.  Documented Gilbert syndrome 
is allowed if total bilirubin is mildly elevated (<6 mg/dL). 
e. Alanine aminotransferase (ALT) and aspartate 
aminotransferase (AST) ≤2.5 × ULN (≤3 × ULN for 
participants with liver involvement of their cancer). 
f. Serum albumin >2 g/dL. 
g. Glomerular filtration rate (GFR) ≥60 mL/min/1.73 m2 either 
directly measured via 24-hour urine collection or calculated 
using the CKD-EPI formula for adults. 
9. Participants and their partners should practice contraception and 
reproduction restrictions of the study, as follows: 
a. Female participants of non-childbearing potential (ie, 
surgically sterile with a hysterectomy and/or bilateral 
oophorectomy; or chemically sterile; or ≥12 months of 
amenorrhea in the absence of chemotherapy, anti-estrogens, 
or ovarian suppression) do not need to undergo pregnancy 
testing. 
b. Female participants of child-bearing potential must have a 
documented negative pregnancy test within 7 days prior to 
starting TYRA-300. 
c. Female participants of child-bearing potential and all male 
participants must agree to use highly effective contraception 

===PAGE 14===

Protocol TYR300-101

Confidential

prior to study entry and up to 180 days after the last dose of 
TYRA-300. 
10. Coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) testing per local standards 
and regulations, and fulfills COVID-19 vaccination requirements 
as per local site regulations (if any).  Participants with a positive 
test result for COVID-19 infection at Screening who fulfil all 
other study eligibility criteria can be rescreened following test 
normalization and clinical recovery. 
Inclusion Criteria for Phase 1, Part B: 
1. Male and female participants who are 18 years of age or older on 
the day of signing the ICF. 
2. Life expectancy >12 weeks. 
3. Ability to understand and sign the ICF and comply with study 
procedures. 
4. ECOG PS ≤1. 
5. Participants with any histologically confirmed advanced solid 
tumor who have exhausted standard therapeutic options. 
a. The tumor must have an eligible FGFR3 gene mutation or 
fusion (see Appendix 14.3.1 and 14.3.2) diagnosed by any 
authorized/approved or validated local test performed in a 
CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory. 
i. When tissue is not available to perform a validated local 
test at a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) 
laboratory, and prior NGS testing has not been performed, 
a central test, performed at a CLIA-certified (or regional 
equivalent) laboratory or a locally validated or approved 
assay, may be used to identify eligible mutations in 
ctDNA in plasma.  
b. A specimen must be submitted for analysis for central ctDNA 
testing, but the results of the test do not need to be available 
before starting TYRA-300. 
c. Any number of prior therapies, including prior FGFR 
inhibitors, are permitted. 
d. Twice daily dose cohorts opened in Part B will enroll 
participants with locally advanced/metastatic FGFR3 
mutation positive urothelial carcinoma who have not received

Inclusion Criteria for Phase 2: 
1. Male and female participants who are 18 years of age or older on 
the day of signing the ICF.

5. Participants must have a histologically confirmed locally 
advanced/metastatic tumor in 1 of the following categories: 
a. Urothelial carcinoma with an eligible FGFR3 gene mutation 
or rearrangement who have progressed on a prior FGFR 
inhibitor and presence of a resistance mutation or other kinase 
domain mutation likely to respond to TYRA-300 identified 
using an FDA authorized/approved CDx or a validated local 
test performed in a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) 
laboratory.  When tissue to perform a validated local test (at a 
CLIA or regional equivalent certified laboratory) is not 
available, a central laboratory assay, performed at a 
CLIA-certified laboratory, or locally validated or approved 
assay, may be used to identify eligible mutations in ctDNA in 
plasma. 
b. Urothelial carcinoma with an eligible FGFR3 gene mutation 
or rearrangement identified by an FDA authorized/approved 
CDx or a CLIA (or regional equivalent) validated local test 
performed in a certified laboratory, who has not received a

Study Exclusion Criteria: 
1. Participant received chemotherapy, targeted therapy, 
immunotherapy, or an investigational therapy within 4 weeks or 
5 half-lives, whichever is shorter, before the first dose of study 
drug (within 6 weeks for nitrosoureas and mitomycin). 
2. Participant has not recovered from reversible toxicity of prior 
anticancer therapy (except toxicities that are not clinically 
significant including, but not limited to, alopecia, skin 
discoloration, or Grade 1 neuropathy). 
3. Had major surgery within 4 weeks prior to enrollment. 
4. Any reason that, in the view of the investigator, would 
substantially impair the ability of the participant to comply with 
study procedures and increase the risk to the participant.  
Examples include poorly controlled diabetes (glycated 
hemoglobin [HbA1c] >8%) and ongoing active infection 
requiring intravenous (IV) antibiotics. 
5. Females who are pregnant, breastfeeding, or planning to become 
pregnant within 180 days after the last dose of TYRA-300 and 
males who plan to father a child while enrolled in this study or 
within 180 days after the last dose of TYRA-300. 
6. Has impaired wound healing capacity defined as skin/decubitus 
ulcers, chronic leg ulcers, known gastric ulcers, or unhealed 
incisions. 
7. Has a serum phosphorus level >ULN during screening (within 
14 days of treatment and prior to Cycle 1, Day 1) that remains 
>ULN despite medical management with phosphate binders. 
8. Any ocular condition likely to increase the risk of eye toxicity, 
including: 
a. History of or current evidence of central serous retinopathy 
(CSR; including ≥Grade 2 CSR while receiving a prior FGFR 
inhibitor) or retinal vascular occlusion. 
b. Active wet, age-related macular degeneration. 
c. Diabetic retinopathy with macular edema. 
d. Uncontrolled glaucoma (per local standard of care). 

===PAGE 20===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 20 of 167 
9. History of or current uncontrolled cardiovascular disease 
including: 
a. Unstable angina, myocardial infarction, or known congestive 
heart failure Class II to IV within the preceding 12 months. 
b. Cerebrovascular accident or transient ischemic attack within 
the preceding 3 months. 
c. Pulmonary embolism within the preceding 2 months. 
10. MRI of the brain must be performed at screening for all patients 
and, if untreated/newly diagnosed brain metastases are identified, 
the candidate is not eligible. 
a. The candidate may rescreen for the study after the definitive 
treatment of brain metastases. 
b. Prior brain metastases treated at least 4 weeks prior to signing 
the full-study ICF or that are clinically and radiographically 
stable for at least 1 month prior to Cycle 1, Day 1 and do not 
require chronic corticosteroid treatment are allowed. 
11. Gastrointestinal disorders that will affect oral administration or 
absorption of TYRA-300. 
12. Known history of human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) 
infection, or active hepatitis B or C infection. 
a. Participants with a history of HIV on antiviral therapy with 
undetectable viral load by polymerase chain reaction (PCR) 
are allowed. 
b. Participants with a history of hepatitis B virus infection with 
positive hepatitis B surface antibody, or positive hepatitis B 
core antibody with a negative PCR test, are allowed.  
i. Participants positive for hepatitis B surface antibody, who 
have received the HBV vaccine, have no history of HBV 
infection, and who are negative for hepatitis B core 
antibody are allowed. 
c. Participants with hepatitis C infection previously treated with 
antiviral therapy and test negative for hepatitis C virus by 
PCR are allowed.

Study Drug(s): 
TYRA-300 capsules and tablets 
Primary Endpoints: 
Phase 1 (Parts A and B): Incidence of DLT events during the DLT 
evaluation period (Cycle 1 [28 days]). 
Phase 2: Investigator-assessed ORR, defined as either a CR or PR by 
RECIST v1.1. 
Secondary 
Endpoints: 
Phase 1 (Parts A and B) and Phase 2: 
• Incidence of AEs characterized by study phase, cohort, 
seriousness, relationship to study drug, timing, and severity. 
• Changes in clinical laboratory parameters, vital signs, 
electrocardiogram (ECG) parameters, and physical 
examination status. 
• Single-dose and steady-state PK parameters, including but not 
limited to accumulation ratio, Cmax, Tmax, AUC0-last, AUCTau, 
AUC0-∞(after first dose only), Vd/F, CL/F, and t1/2. 
 
Additional secondary endpoint for Phase 1, Part B only: 
• ORR.

Safety Outcome 
Measures: 
Safety will be assessed by close monitoring and timely assessment of 
AEs, laboratory parameters (hematology and clinical chemistry), vital 
signs (blood pressure and heart rate), participant’s medical condition 
(physical examination including weight), ophthalmological 
examination, and general wellbeing and activities of daily life 
(ECOG PS). 
Statistical 
Considerations: 
Analysis Sets: 
• The Safety Analysis Set (SAS) will include participants who 
receive at least 1 dose of TYRA-300.  All safety analyses will be 
based on the SAS. 
• The Phase 1 Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set (EAS1) will include 
all participants enrolled in Phase 1 who receive at least 1 dose of 
TYRA-300 and have a baseline disease (imaging) assessment. 
• For sensitivity analysis, a second Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set 
for Phase 1 (EAS1a) will include all participants who received at 
least one dose of TYRA-300, have a baseline disease (imaging) 
assessment, and have an eligible FGFR3 alteration by dose level. 
CCI
CCI
CCI

===PAGE 23===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 23 of 167 
• The Phase 2 Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set (EAS2) will include 
all participants enrolled in Phase 2, Cohorts 1 or 2 who have 
FGFR3 gene alterations confirmed by a sponsor-designated 
central assay, receive at least 1 dose of TYRA-300, and have a 
baseline disease (imaging) assessment. 
• For sensitivity analysis, a secondary Efficacy-evaluable Analysis 
Set for Phase 2 (EAS2a) will include all participants enrolled in 
Cohorts 1 or 2, regardless of whether or not FGFR3 gene 
alterations are confirmed by a sponsor-designated central assay, 
who receive at least 1 dose of TYRA-300, and have a baseline 
disease (imaging) assessment. 
• The EAS1, EAS1a, EAS2, and EAS2a will be used for response 
analysis and efficacy related data. 
• The DLT-evaluable Analysis Set (Phase 1 only) will include all 
participants enrolled in Phase 1 for dose escalation purposes who 
experience a DLT within the DLT evaluation period or those 
without a DLT, but who complete safety assessments through the 
DLT evaluation period and receive at least 75% of the planned 
total dose during that time.  Non-DLT-evaluable participants will 
be replaced.  The DLT-evaluable Analysis Set will be used for 
the analysis of MTD. 
• The PK Analysis Set will include all participants who receive at 
least 1 dose of study drug and have at least 1 postdose PK blood 
sample collected.  Additional participants may be excluded from 
the PK Analysis Set at the discretion of the pharmacokineticist.  
All PK analyses will be based on the PK Analysis Set. 
Methodology: 
• Detailed methodology for summary and statistical analyses of the 
data collected in this study will be documented in a Statistical 
Analysis Plan. 
• Data will be summarized using descriptive statistics (number of 
participants, mean, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum) 
for continuous variables and using frequencies and percentages 
for discrete variables.  Data will be summarized by phase, dose, 
part, and cohort unless otherwise specified.  Safety and tumor 
response data will be compared over time to assess change from 
baseline during treatment (baseline is defined as the screening 
tumor assessment for tumor response).  DL differences will be 

===PAGE 24===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 24 of 167 
assessed using appropriate parametric or non-parametric 
significance tests, as applicable. 
Safety Analyses: 
• Safety will be assessed through summaries of DLTs, AEs, 
changes in laboratory test results, changes in vital signs and 
ECGs, and exposure to TYRA-300. 
 
Efficacy Analyses: 
• ORR determined by the investigator will be assessed using 
RECIST v1.1.  ORR is defined as the proportion of participants 
with a CR or PR.  Participants with missing or no response 
assessments will be classified as non-responders.  The point 
estimates of the ORR and 2-sided 95% exact confidence intervals 
(CI) will be presented by dose in Phase 1 Part B and by cohort in 
Phase 2.  Other antitumor activity endpoints will be analyzed and 
summarized as appropriate: DOR, DCR, TTR, PFS, and OS.  
Time-to-event variables (DOR, PFS, and OS) will be summarized 
using Kaplan Meier methods and figures for the estimated median 
time. 
Pharmacokinetic and Pharmacodynamic Analyses: 
• PK data will be analyzed using PhoenixTM WinNonlin® 
(Version 8.1 or higher).  If data warrant, the relationship between 
PK and pharmacodynamic parameters will be explored.

1 INTRODUCTION AND RATIONALE 
TYRA-300 is a novel, potent, fibroblast growth factor receptor (FGFR) 3-selective tyrosine 
kinase inhibitor (TKI) that is being developed to treat advanced solid tumors, including urothelial 
carcinoma, that contain activating gene alterations of FGFR3. 
1.1 
Background 
1.1.1 
Fibroblast Growth Factor Receptor Biology 
The fibroblast growth factor (FGF) family of receptor tyrosine kinases is composed of 4 highly 
conserved membrane-associated proteins (FGFR1, FGFR2, FGFR3, and FGFR4) that function to 
regulate cellular growth, differentiation, and homeostasis.  The structures of the FGFRs contain 
an extracellular domain, transmembrane domain, and an intracellular tyrosine kinase domain.  
The activity of the intracellular kinase domain is modulated by interactions of the extracellular 
domain with members of the FGF family of ligands.  Twenty-two FGF family members have 
been identified; of these, 18 are secreted and act as ligands for the FGFRs.  Most of these FGFs 
act locally, in a paracrine fashion, to affect neighboring cells, while 3 FGFs (FGF19, FGF21, and 
FGF23) circulate in the blood and act in an endocrine fashion to regulate FGFRs in distant 
tissues.  When activated, these receptors stimulate multiple cellular pathways including 
proliferation.1 
The 4 FGFRs are structurally homologous but have specific physiologic roles depending on their 
tissue expression and ligand interactions.  The functions of these receptors are often redundant 
and overlapping, and include roles in embryogenesis, tissue homeostasis, tissue repair, apoptosis, 
and cellular migration.2  Germline mutations in the receptors can be associated with genetic 
disorders (eg, a point mutation in FGFR3 can lead to achondroplasia, the most common form of 
dwarfism in humans). 
1.1.2 
Fibroblast Growth Factor Receptors in Cancer 
Our understanding of the role of FGFR signaling in cancer continues to evolve.  In the FGFR 
family of receptors, oncogenic alterations can occur in the ligand-binding and transmembrane 
domains, acting as constitutive activators, as well as directly in the intracellular kinase domain.  
Likewise, in addition to mutations, oncogenesis can occur in the FGFRs through gene 
rearrangements and gene amplifications.  Activating mutations, fusions, or amplifications trigger 
multiple intracellular pathways, such as the mitogen-activated protein kinase and 
phosphoinositide 3-kinase pathways, that are common with other oncogenes.3  Using 
next-generation sequencing (NGS) it has been shown that 7.1% of cancers have some form of 
FGFR alteration with gene amplification being the most common (66%), followed by activating 
mutations (26%).  The most common cancers manifesting mutations appear to be urothelial 
(32%), breast (18%), endometrial (13%), lung (13%), and ovarian (9%).4 

===PAGE 36===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 36 of 167 
1.1.3 
Fibroblast Growth Factor Receptor-directed Therapies in Cancer 
Multiple FGFR inhibitors are currently in development and 3 have been approved by the United 
States (US) Food and Drug Administration (FDA): erdafitinib for FGFR2 and FGFR3 mutant 
locally advanced or metastatic urothelial carcinoma; and pemigatinib and infigratinib for 
FGFR2-fusion or rearrangement positive intrahepatic cholangiocarcinoma (ICC).  These 
inhibitors have demonstrated clinical benefit; however, response rates and duration of response 
(DOR) are limited.5,6,7  While patients may initially respond to FGFR targeted therapies, many 
develop acquired drug resistance, ultimately resulting in disease progression and discontinuation 
of therapy.  Decreased activity of erdafitinib and pemigatinib due to resistance mutations that 
alter their ability to bind to the active site, such as gatekeeper mutations, has been observed.2,8  
Gatekeeper mutations have also been seen in patients treated with infigratinib in a clinical study 
while acquired-resistance molecular brake mutations have been seen in patients in clinical 
studies of both pemigatinib and infigratinib.9,10 
1.1.4 
Fibroblast Growth Factor Receptor 3 in Urothelial Cancer 
Urothelial carcinoma is a major source of morbidity and mortality worldwide with over 
420,000 new cases annually and over 165,000 deaths.11  FGFR3 alterations have been found in 
up to 80% of non-muscle invasive bladder cancers and up to 15 to 20% of muscle invasive 
tumors.  The most common FGFR3 alteration is a mutation found in the ligand-binding domain, 
S249C, followed by a mutation in the transmembrane domain, Y375C; these 2 mutations account 
for approximately 80% of the FGFR3 mutations in bladder cancer.12 
Erdafitinib, an oral pan-FGFR inhibitor, demonstrated an objective response rate (ORR) of 
32.2% and a median DOR of 5.4 months in urothelial tumors with specific FGFR3 or FGFR2 
gene alterations.5  Based on these results, erdafitinib received accelerated approval in the US for 
the treatment of FGFR3- or FGFR2-positive urothelial carcinoma that has progressed during or 
following at least 1 line of prior platinum-containing chemotherapy including within 12 months 
of neoadjuvant or adjuvant platinum-containing chemotherapy.13  Janssen Research and 
Development, LLC recently reported positive results of a Phase 3 confirmatory study for 
erdafitinib (NCT03390504).  Patients with FGFR3 or FGFR2 gene alterations were randomized 
1:1 to either erdafitinib or investigator’s choice chemotherapy (vinflunine or docetaxel) with a 
primary endpoint of overall survival (OS).14  With a median follow up of 15.9 months, 
erdafitinib demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful OS benefit compared 
to chemotherapy (12.1 months vs. 7.8 months; HR 0.64; 95% CI, 0.47 to 0.88; P=0.005) and 
well as the secondary endpoint of PFS (5.6 months vs. 2.7 months; HR 0.58; 95% CI, 0.44 to 
0.78l P<0.001).  These results demonstrate the survival benefit of inhibiting specific FGFR 
mutations in metastatic urothelial cancer with targeted therapy. 14  Other pan-FGFR inhibitors 
have also been evaluated in urothelial carcinoma including infigratinib, rogaratinib, pemigatinib, 
and ABSK091 (AZD4547). 
Among the key limitations of all late-stage FGFR inhibitors, including erdafitinib, is the 
emergence of mutations like the gatekeeper mutation.9  In a study of infigratinib and other FGFR 
inhibitors, the gatekeeper mutation has been described as a valine to methionine change at the 
V555 position of FGFR3, which results in a significant shift in potency of all of the first 
generation FGFR inhibitors.  In preclinical studies, erdafitinib was ineffective in an in vivo 

===PAGE 37===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 37 of 167 
model harboring the V555M gatekeeper mutation.15  Once patients progress due to acquired drug 
resistance, there are very few options available, representing a significant unmet need in this 
patient population. 
Another limitation of current FGFR inhibitors is the lack of isoform specificity.  Erdafitinib is a 
pan-FGFR inhibitor and, due to its lack of selectivity, there may be toxicities associated with the 
inhibition of FGFR1, 2, and 4.  FGFR1 is expressed in kidney cells where it regulates phosphate 
and calcium reabsorption, and inhibition of FGFR1 results in hyperphosphatemia.1  
Hyperphosphatemia was the dose-limiting toxicity (DLT) and was reported in over 70% of 
patients in a Phase 2 clinical study of erdafitinib.  Hyperphosphatemia and other toxicities 
contributed to dose reductions in 56% of patients, which may ultimately limit target engagement 
and efficacy.5  A similarly high rate of FGFR-related toxicities has been reported in clinical 
studies of other non-selective FGFR inhibitors including pemigatinib, infigratinib and 
futibatinib.6,7,16 
1.2 
Study Rationale 
Current approved and late-stage pan-FGFR inhibitors are susceptible to acquired resistance 
mutations at the gatekeeper position of the FGFR3 protein.  These same pan-FGFR inhibitors are 
limited by significant toxicity related to inhibiting off-target receptor tyrosine kinases including 
other isoforms of the FGFR family.  This off-target activity limits dosing and theoretically 
provides further opportunities for acquired resistance to occur due to frequent dose interruptions 
and dose reductions.  TYRA-300, a highly selective inhibitor of FGFR3, is in development for 
the treatment of FGFR3-driven cancers.  Resistance to approved and investigational FGFR 
inhibitors has been shown to arise due to mutations in the gatekeeper region of FGFR3.9  
TYRA-300 was designed to avoid this region of FGFR3 and, in preclinical models, TYRA-300 
has demonstrated similar potency against both wild-type and resistant FGFR3 targets.15 
The TYR300-101 study is to be conducted in compliance with the protocol, Good Clinical 
Practice (GCP) guidelines, and applicable regulatory requirements.

1.4 
Study Endpoint Rationale 
The incidence of DLT events over a period of 28 days (representing a single cycle of treatment 
with study drug), with dose escalation via an interval 3 + 3 (i3 + 3) design, is considered 
appropriate to determine the maximum tolerated dose (MTD).18  In Phase 1, Parts A and B, 
safety and tolerability will also be assessed using standard measures employed throughout 
clinical studies, such as the incidence of adverse events (AEs) and changes in clinical laboratory 
parameters, vital signs, electrocardiogram (ECG) parameters, and physical examination status.  
In addition to MTD, additional measures of pharmacodynamic engagement, such as changes in 
, changes in FGFR signaling in tissue, reduction in circulating tumor DNA 
(ctDNA), Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors version 1.1 (RECIST v1.1) responses, 
and the emergence of longer-term toxicity will be used to determine the recommended Phase 2 
dose (RP2D) of TYRA-300. 
ORR in single arm trials is correlated with both progression-free survival (PFS) and overall 
survival (OS) in randomized clinical trials of targeted therapies and is likely to predict clinical 
benefit in single arm studies.19  ORR is the primary endpoint for Phase 2 and a secondary 
endpoint for Phase 1, Part B.  ORR is rigorously defined by RECIST v1.1 criteria and is an 
endpoint widely accepted by regulatory agencies as a measure of clinical benefit.20  The 
secondary endpoints of DOR, disease control rate (DCR), time to response (TTR; Phase 1, Part B 
and Phase 2), and PFS (Cohorts 1 and 2 of Phase 2 only) are commonly used in oncology studies 
to support ORR data.

1.5 
Risks and Benefits for Participants 
Activating FGFR3 gene alterations are most commonly observed in urothelial carcinoma and are 
present in 15 to 20% of metastatic cases.  Pan-FGFR inhibitors have demonstrated clinical 
benefit in FGFR3-driven metastatic urothelial cancer with ORRs between 25% and 40%, 
including an accelerated approval for erdafitinib by the US FDA.5,9,21  However, first generation 
pan-FGFR inhibitors have a number of limitations.  Acquired resistance mutations have been 
reported to occur at the gatekeeper position V555 in the kinase domain and were associated with 
disease progression.  
The safety profile for currently approved and late-stage FGFR inhibitors are well-defined, and 
non-FGFR3-associated activity is associated with significant toxicity and limits dosing.  For 
example, hyperphosphatemia, mediated by inhibition of FGFR1, is a common AE and the DLT 
for erdafitinib, pemigatinib, and infigratinib, the 3 approved pan-FGFR inhibitors.  Inhibition of 
bile acid metabolism mediated by FGFR4 results in gastrointestinal irritation and diarrhea due to 
increased bile acids in the feces.  Common toxicities that result in dose interruptions and 
reductions, such as stomatitis, have been observed with the FGFR2b antibody bemarituzumab 
and the FGFR2-selective TKI RLY-4008, and appear to be associated with FGFR2 
inhibition.22,23  Pan-FGFR inhibitors also result in common toxicities of the skin and nails, which 
may be mediated by inhibition of FGFR2 as well.24 
TYRA-300 is a highly selective FGFR3-inhibitor that has been designed to specifically address 
acquired resistance due to mutations at the kinase domain gatekeeper position.  There may be a 
therapeutic benefit of limiting inhibition of FGFR1, FGFR2, and FGFR4, and allowing for more 
potent inhibition of FGFR3 in urothelial cancer and other FGFR3-driven tumors.  FGFR3 plays a 
major role in cartilage differentiation and bone development in children, and FGFR3-selective 
inhibition may alter bone development, though in children and adults with closed growth plates, 
these effects are likely to be minimal.  With respect to other drugs that selectively target FGFR3, 
the monoclonal antibody vofatamab, in combination with docetaxel, was well-tolerated and 
demonstrated toxicity primarily associated with the chemotherapy.25  These results indicate that 
the DLTs for TYRA-300 are likely to be those associated with other pan-FGFR inhibitors at the 
highest planned doses in this study, and the potential benefit-risk ratio is favorable for 
participants enrolled to this study. 
1.5.1 
Risks and Benefits for Participants During the Coronavirus Disease 2019 
Pandemic 
The European Society for Medical Oncology (ESMO) have emphasized the importance of 
continuing cancer research in a clinical study setting during the coronavirus disease 2019 
(COVID-19) pandemic, as cancer remains a significant cause of morbidity and mortality, and 
that failure to invest in ongoing and future clinical studies may lead to ‘collateral damage’ from 
COVID-19.26 
In the general population, vaccination protects against severe acute respiratory syndrome 
coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) infection, hospitalization, and death.27,28  The benefits of 
COVID-19 vaccination have similarly been reported in patients with cancer.  In a cohort of 
cancer patients constructed from 50 US medical centers in the National Center for Advancing 

===PAGE 40===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 40 of 167 
Translational Services’ National COVID Cohort Collaborative (N3C), there was an increased 
risk of all-cause mortality in patients with a COVID-19 positive diagnosis compared with 
patients who had a COVID-19 negative diagnosis (hazard ratio: 1.20; 95% confidence interval 
(CI): 1.15 to 1.24; P <0.001) after adjusting for all other potential risk factors.29  An 
observational, prospective study of ambulatory patients with cancer with asymptomatic or 
pauci-symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection found that serious adverse events (SAEs) were more 
frequent (12.4% versus 5.5%; P = 0.0008; driven by pneumonitis) and mortality was higher 
(8/89 versus 3/204; P = 0.004) in patients with a positive versus negative SARS-CoV-2 test 
result.30 
Vaccination is considered the most effective preventative measure against COVID-19 infection 
in patients with cancer.31  Patients with cancer may be considered a very high-risk group for 
priority vaccination, in view of the increased risk of mortality with COVID-19 infection 
compared with the general population.32  Consequently, the TYR300-101 study recommends 
eligible participants to fulfil COVID-19 vaccination requirements as per local site regulations (if 
any).  This recommendation is aligned with guidance from oncologic societies such as the 
American Society of Clinical Oncology, the National Comprehensive Cancer Network, ESMO, 
and the Society for Immunotherapy of Cancer, which recommend the COVID-19 vaccine to 
patients with active cancer regardless of therapy.33,34,35,36

2 STUDY OBJECTIVES 
2.1 
Primary Objectives 
The primary objectives of this study are: 
• To determine the optimal and MTDs, and RP2D of TYRA-300 in participants with advanced 
solid tumors (Phase 1, Parts A and B). 
• To evaluate the preliminary antitumor activity of TYRA-300 at the RP2D in participants in 
selected tumor expansion cohorts with activating FGFR3 gene alterations (Phase 2). 
2.2 
Secondary Objectives 
The secondary objectives of this study are: 
• To characterize the safety and tolerability of TYRA-300 in participants with advanced cancer 
(Phase 1, Parts A and B, and Phase 2). 
• To conduct a preliminary characterization of the pharmacokinetics (PK) and 
pharmacodynamics in participants treated with TYRA-300 (Phase 1, Parts A and B, and 
Phase 2). 
• To characterize ORR in participants with activating FGFR3 gene alterations (Phase 1, 
Part B). 
• To characterize DOR (Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2). 
• To characterize DCR >12 weeks (Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2). 
• To characterize TTR (Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2). 
• To characterize PFS in participants treated with TYRA-300 in specific tumor expansion 
cohorts (Cohorts 1 and 2 of Phase 2 only). 
2.3 
Exploratory Objectives

3 STUDY ENDPOINTS 
3.1 
Primary Endpoints 
The primary endpoint for Phase 1 (Parts A and B) is: 
• Incidence of DLT events during the DLT evaluation period (Cycle 1 [28 days]). 
The primary endpoint for Phase 2 is: 
• Investigator-assessed ORR, defined as either a complete response (CR) or a partial response 
(PR) by RECIST v1.1. 
3.2 
Secondary Endpoints 
The secondary endpoints for Phase 1 (Parts A and B) and Phase 2 are: 
• Incidence of AEs characterized by study phase, cohort, seriousness, relationship to study 
drug, timing, and severity. 
• Changes in clinical laboratory parameters, vital signs, ECG parameters, and physical 
examination status. 
• Single-dose and steady-state PK parameters, including but not limited to accumulation ratio, 
Cmax, Tmax, AUC0-last, AUCTau, AUC0-∞(after first dose only), Vd/F, CL/F, and t1/2. 
Additional secondary endpoint for Phase 1, Part B only: 
• ORR. 
Additional secondary endpoints for Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2: 
• DOR. 
• DCR, defined as CR, PR, or stable disease (SD) for >12 weeks. 
• TTR. 
Additional secondary endpoint for Phase 2 only: 
• PFS in Cohorts 1 and 2.

4 STUDY PLAN 
4.1 
Study Design 
This multicenter, open-label clinical study is designed to evaluate TYRA-300 in participants 
with locally advanced/metastatic urothelial carcinoma and other solid tumors with 
FGFR3-activating gene alterations.  The study will be conducted in several parts (Figure 4.1). 
An Informed Consent Form (ICF) must be signed by the participant before any study-related 
procedures are performed.

4.1.1 
Phase 1, Part A: Dose Escalation 
Participants with any advanced solid tumor for which there are no approved standard therapies 
are eligible to be enrolled.  For dose levels above 120 mg daily, participants must have an 
eligible FGFR3 gene mutation or fusion diagnosed by any authorized/approved or validated local 
test performed in a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory facility.  The TYRA-300 
starting dose in Part A will be 10 mg daily (DL 1), with an anticipated dose escalation schema as 
shown in Table 4.1. 
Table 4.1:

Abbreviations: BID = twice daily, DL = dose level, PK = pharmacokinetics, PO = by mouth, QD = once daily, 
SRC = safety review committee. 
The study will use an i3 + 3 design with a target toxicity rate of the MTD of 0.3 (0.25 to 0.35).18  
Each dose level (DL) will be assessed through the DLT evaluation period (Cycle 1 [28 days]) 
before the next DL can be enrolled.  Appendix 14.2 shows the i3 + 3 decisions by number of 
participants and number of toxicities.  Depending on the number of participants and the number 
of DLTs observed, the decision will be to escalate to a higher dose or, if at DL6, continue 
enrollment in the highest dose (E), stay at the same dose (S), de-escalate to the previous lower 
dose (D), or de-escalate to the previous lower dose and never repeat current dose (DU).  Dose 
escalation decisions will be made by the recommendation of the Safety Review Committee 
(SRC), comprising site investigators, the study medical monitor, the study statistician, and the 
sponsor medical monitor.  Please refer to Section 4.1.4 and the SRC charter for further details. 
DL1 and DL2 will proceed in an accelerated fashion, monitoring toxicity in 1 participant during 
the DLT evaluation period.  Any ≥Grade 2 treatment-related adverse event (TRAE) will 
constitute a DLT for DL1 and DL2.  If no ≥Grade 2 TRAE occurs at DL1, the next participant 
will be enrolled to DL2.  Similarly, if no ≥Grade 2 TRAE occurs at DL2, the next cohort will 
dose escalate (ie, enrolled to DL3).  If either DL1 or DL2 enroll additional participants due to 
this stricter DLT definition, the study will convert to an i3 + 3 design and all subsequent dose 
escalation decisions will use the same DLT definitions as for DL3 and above (ie, no longer use 
≥Grade 2 TRAEs as the definition for DLTs). 

===PAGE 47===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 47 of 167 
DL3 and above will enroll a minimum of 3 participants per DL.  These cohorts may enroll 1 to 
3 additional participants at a time if required by the DLT rules.  Dose escalation will continue up 
to DL6, until the MTD is determined, or until approximately 30 participants have been enrolled 
in Part A.  Within each DL in Phase 1, Part A, dosing of the first 2 participants will be staggered 
such that the second participant may not be dosed on the same day as the first participant. 
4.1.2 
Phase 1, Part B: Dose Expansion 
When DL3 has cleared dose escalation, dose expansion cohorts will begin enrolling in parallel to 
further explore pharmacodynamics 
 in FGFR3-mutated cancers.  Enrollment will 
be limited to participants with advanced/metastatic solid tumors with an FGFR3 activating gene 
alteration (see Appendix 14.3 for eligible gene alterations) for which an FGFR-directed therapy 
is appropriate.  Participants with an eligible gene alteration, based on an authorized/approved 
validated local test, performed at a Clinical Laboratory Improvement Amendments (CLIA; or 
regional equivalent) certified laboratory, may be enrolled and a specimen must be submitted for 
testing with the sponsor-designated central assay.  Candidates for the study with metastatic 
urothelial carcinoma may undergo prescreening with a central assay performed at a 
CLIA-certified laboratory, or a locally validated or approved assay, to identify eligible mutations 
in ctDNA in plasma to identify eligible FGFR3 gene alterations if tissue is not available for a 
validated local test at a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory and no prior NGS 
testing has been performed (see Appendix 14.14 for prescreening eligibility).  Participants in 
dose expansion will undergo additional analyses for 
pharmacodynamic endpoints 
including, but not limited to, on treatment and post-progression assessment of ctDNA, serial 
, and skin biopsies to assess FGFR target inhibition. 
Part B will also utilize the i3 + 3 design, but dose escalation will not apply.  The cohort can 
continue expansion based on the toxicity probability resulting from participants observed in both 
Parts A and B at that DL.  Because Part A will be dose-escalating to DL4 through DL6 in 
parallel, DLTs observed in Part B may result in a need to pause or stop enrollment into Part A.  
Depending on the number of participants and number of DLTs observed, the decision will be to 
continue Part B expansion (E), continue Part B expansion but pause enrollment in Part A (S), or 
stop expansion to the DL and discontinue Part A (D or DU).  Cohort expansion can continue if 
the toxicity probability is not above 0.35 for the entire DL that includes data from both Parts A 
and B.  If Part B results in a pause to enrollment in Part A, enrollment to higher doses in Part A 
can proceed again if dose expansion and continued monitoring of Part B reduces the toxicity rate 
to less than 0.25.  It is estimated that up to 50 participants will be enrolled in Part B. 
When doses approaching the MTD are identified in Part A, BID dose cohorts may be explored in 
Part B based on emerging PK data. The BID dosing cohorts will enroll participants with locally 
advanced/metastatic FGFR3 mutation positive urothelial carcinoma who have not received a 
prior FGFR inhibitor. The anticipated dose expansion cohorts are shown in Table 4.2.  
Twice daily dose cohorts will evaluate the PK of a tablet formulation that is to be used in the 
Phase 2 part of the study.  PK and AEs will be reviewed by the SRC after 3 to 5 participants in 
new BID expansion cohorts to ensure there are no safety concerns before completing enrolment 
of the dose level.

with eligible FGFR3 alterations, are allowed with medical monitor approval. 
4.1.3 
Intra-participant Dose Escalation Decisions – Phase 1, Part A and B 
Intra-participant dose escalation will be allowed for participants in these cohorts under the 
following circumstances: 
• A higher dose has been cleared in dose escalation (Part A) and was deemed to be tolerated. 
• The participant has not had a >Grade 1 TRAE while taking TYRA-300. 
• If a ≥Grade 3 treatment-related toxicity in any participant occurs at a given DL, 
intra-participant dose escalation is not allowed at that DL. 
• In the opinion of the investigator, and with approval from the medical monitor, a higher dose 
under study in an enrolling or completed cohort may be safe and potentially more effective in 
treating the participant’s tumor than the current dose. 
• The participant has completed at least 2 cycles of study drug and have at least 1 postbaseline 
tumor imaging assessment. 
Participants with evidence of progressive disease (PD), as defined by RECIST v1.1, may 
continue to receive study drug if, in the opinion of the investigator and with approval from the 

===PAGE 49===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 49 of 167 
medical monitor, the participant is deriving benefit from continuing study drug.  The following 
criteria must be met in order to provide assurance that the participant is not exposed to an 
unreasonable risk: 
• Absence of symptoms and signs indicating clinically significant progression of disease. 
• No decline in Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group Performance Status (ECOG PS) from 
baseline. 
• Absence of symptomatic rapid disease progression requiring urgent medical intervention (eg, 
symptomatic pleural effusion, spinal cord compression, etc). 
o Patients who have experienced disease progression in the CNS but have stable disease or 
better in the periphery, may continue to receive treatment with TYRA-300 after definitive 
treatment of the CNS lesions. 
At the time of radiographic progression, participants must also be reconsented and provided with 
details of all approved therapies, and potential clinical benefit, that the participant may be 
foregoing in order to continue receiving TYRA-300. 
4.1.4 
Safety Review Committee 
The sponsor and study investigators will constitute an SRC and will meet routinely to assess 
participant safety.  As defined by the SRC charter, dose escalation decisions will be based on the 
recommendations of the SRC.  Reports will be presented to the SRC as per the SRC charter.  
Doses will be increased no more than 50% of the previous dose beyond DL3 and intermediate 
doses may be explored.  The MTD will be the highest DL with no more than 35% of participants 
who have an AE that meets the criteria for a DLT.  A participant may be replaced if they do not 
receive at least 75% of the planned dose during the DLT evaluation period for reasons other than 
a TRAE or if they discontinue the study for non-DLT reasons during the DLT evaluation period. 
4.1.5 
Dose Escalation Decisions – Phase 1, Part A and B 
Before escalating to the next dose in the study (Part A), the SRC will hold a safety data review 
meeting.  An interim safety report prepared by the medical monitor/investigator (for early reports 
of few [eg, <12] participants) or the data manager/statistician (for later reports with more 
participants) will be reviewed.  Reports may include PK, AEs, vital signs, ECGs, and clinical 
laboratory safety results after data are available through Cycle 2, Day 1 in the study for the 
current dose cohort.  This will be reviewed together with any emerging safety findings from 
earlier treatment groups. 
If total DLTs at a DL exceed 35% due to DLTs in an expansion cohort, the SRC will convene to 
determine if an interim safety report is needed and how to proceed with Part A given the overall 
DLT rate in a previous DL. 
In general, dose escalation or expansion will proceed according to the i3 + 3 decision table in 
Appendix 14.2. 

===PAGE 50===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 50 of 167 
If any of the participant/study stopping criteria (Section 4.2) are met within a dose cohort, further 
enrollment into that cohort will not proceed until all safety data from participants at that dose are 
reviewed by the SRC.  If the decision is made not to initiate a new cohort before all planned prior 
cohorts are completed, it will not be regarded as premature study termination.  Following the 
review of safety data, one of the following recommendations will be made: 
• To continue with the study as planned (ie, there are no significant safety concerns) or with 
appropriate study amendments to address emerging safety risks. 
• To continue with the study but at a dose between the current dose and the next planned dose, 
or at a dose between the current dose and the previous lower dose. 
• To continue with the study but expand either the current cohort, or a higher/lower dose 
cohort, by an appropriate number of participants for more detailed safety evaluation. 
• To terminate the study. 
In the event a participant/study stopping criterion is met, a written statement will be provided to 
inform the Institutional Review Board (IRB)/Ethics Committee (EC), including which criteria 
were met and the final decision regarding dose escalation or continued dosing.  This statement 
may include information from the review of safety data. 
4.1.6 
Definition of Dose-limiting Toxicities 
For an AE to qualify as a DLT it must be causally related to TYRA-300 (not all related AEs are 
regarded as a DLT).  A DLT will be defined as any of the following occurring during Cycle 1 of 
a DL and regarded by the investigators and/or sponsor to be related to TYRA-300.  The 
Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) v5.0 will be used to assess 
toxicities/AEs. 
Hematological Dose-limiting Toxicities 
• Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) <500/mm3 for >7 days. 
• Febrile neutropenia (a disorder characterized by an ANC <1,000/mm3 and a single 
temperature 38.3°C or a sustained temperature of 38.0°C for >1 hour); or Grade 3 neutropenia 
that lasts for >7 days with or without drug intervention. 
• Platelets <25,000/mm3. 
• Grade 3 hemorrhage associated with thrombocytopenia of ≥Grade 3. 
Non-hematologic Dose-limiting Toxicities 
• Non-hematologic Grade 3 or Grade 4 toxicity, including nausea and vomiting refractory to 
antiemetics.  The following are not considered DLTs: 
o Grade 3 nausea or vomiting of <48 hours duration. 

===PAGE 51===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 51 of 167 
o Grade 3 fatigue for <1 week. 
o Grade 3 or higher electrolyte abnormality (excluding hyperphosphatemia) that lasts for 
≤72 hours, is not clinically complicated, and resolves spontaneously or responds to 
conventional medical interventions. 
o Grade 3 or higher amylase or lipase elevation that is not associated with symptoms or 
clinical or radiological manifestations of pancreatitis. 
• 
Grade 3 hyperphosphatemia (severe or medically significant but not immediately 
life-threatening; hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization indicated) 
regardless of resolution with phosphate binders will be considered a DLT. 
• Hy’s law: 
o Aspartate aminotransferase (AST) or alanine aminotransferase (ALT) >3 × upper limit of 
normal (ULN) and 
o A total bilirubin >2 × ULN and 
o Alkaline phosphatase <2 × ULN and 
o No other reason for liver injury.  
• Any AST or ALT elevation >8 × ULN regardless of duration and AST or ALT evaluation 
5 to 8 × ULN that persists for >2 weeks – all regardless of the presence or absence of liver 
metastasis. 
• Dose delay of >14 days for the study drug due to an AE. 
• Any death not clearly due to the underlying disease or extraneous causes. 
Miscellaneous Dose-limiting Toxicities 
In case an unexpected drug-related toxicity is seen more frequently, this toxicity may be declared 
a DLT for the remainder of the study after thorough consultation between the investigator and 
the sponsor. 
Any toxicity thought to be related to TYRA-300 that, at the discretion of the investigator, or their 
designated associate(s), is thought to warrant withholding the drug for >7 days during the DLT 
evaluation period.  Such toxicities might be Grade 1 or Grade 2 toxicities which interfere with 
the activities of daily life (eg, long-lasting fatigue or anorexia), making a dose 
interruption/reduction necessary in order to ensure the participant’s compliance.  Examples may 
include Grade 2 nail loss or Grade 3 paronychia that does not improve after 4 weeks of 
supportive treatment. 
For certain toxicities such as laboratory assessments without a clear clinical correlation (eg, 
lipase increase without signs of a clinical pancreatitis; or Grade 2 hyperphosphatemia that does 

===PAGE 52===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 52 of 167 
not recover to ≤Grade 1 within 7 days after treating with phosphate binders and diet 
modification), a discussion between the investigator and the sponsor may take place to determine 
if that AE should be assessed as a DLT necessitating dose reduction. 
The first dosing cohorts (DL1 and DL2) will utilize a stricter definition for DLT than DL3 and 
above: any ≥Grade 2 TRAE will constitute a DLT for DL1 and DL2.  If DL1 or DL2 are 
expanded due to this stricter DLT definition, further dose escalation decisions will be made using 
the same DLT definitions used for DL3 and above (ie, no longer use ≥Grade 2 TRAEs as the 
definition for DLTs). 
Results from the dose escalation and dose expansion cohorts will be analyzed for safety, as well 
as potential relationships between dose, PK, 
, and preliminary 
antitumor activity of TYRA-300.  The RP2D will be selected using the totality of the Phase 1 
data. 
4.1.7 
Phase 2: Cohort Expansion 
Once an MTD is determined and an RP2D selected, participants will be enrolled in Phase 2 
expansion cohorts to further characterize preliminary efficacy, safety, and tolerability in the 
following cohorts: 
1. Participants with locally advanced/metastatic urothelial carcinoma previously treated with an 
FGFR inhibitor who now have defined FGFR3 resistance mutations to any FGFR inhibitors: 
o Participants must have had prior approved therapies including platinum-based 
chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an antibody-drug conjugate, unless 
in the opinion of the investigator the participant will not tolerate any of these approved 
agents, or the participant refuses any/all these therapies. 
 For France only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have had prior approved 
therapies including platinum-based chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, 
and an antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the investigator the 
participant will not tolerate these therapies. 
 For Spain only (Appendix 14.15): Participants must have had prior approved 
therapies including platinum-based chemotherapy, an immune checkpoint inhibitor, 
and an antibody-drug conjugate, unless in the opinion of the investigator the 
participant will not tolerate any of these approved agents, or the participant refuses 
these therapies. 
o Participants with urothelial carcinoma will be screened and evaluated for eligible 
resistance mutations using a central assay at a CLIA-certified laboratory, or a locally 
validated assay, that assesses somatic mutations in ctDNA from plasma.  Participants 
with an eligible gene alteration based on an authorized/approved or validated local test, 
performed at a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) laboratory, may be enrolled and a 
specimen must be submitted for central testing using a sponsor-designated central 
laboratory.  Confirmation is not necessary for these participants before starting 
TYRA-300.

Participants in Phase 2 with evidence of PD, as defined by RECIST v1.1, may continue to 
receive study drug if they meet the same criteria as described for Phase 1 (Section 4.1.3). 
For Phase 2, approximately 110 participants will be enrolled in each of Cohorts 1 and 2.  
Candidates with metastatic urothelial carcinoma may undergo prescreening with the 
sponsor-designated central assay, prior to enrolling in the study, to identify eligible FGFR3 gene 
alterations if tissue is not available for a validated local test, performed at a CLIA-certified (or 
regional equivalent) laboratory, and no prior NGS testing has been performed (see 
Appendix 14.14 for prescreening eligibility).  A Simon’s 2-Stage design will be used for 
enrollment.  In the first stage, 39 participants will be accrued and if there are 6 or fewer 
responses, enrollment to the cohort will be stopped for futility.  Otherwise, 71 additional 
participants will be accrued for a total of 110 participants.  Responses are defined as CR or PR 
by RECIST v1.1 at any time while on therapy; for Cohort 1, clinical benefit (prolonged SD of 
≥6 months while on therapy) will count as a response. 
Cohort 3 is exploratory, and approximately 40 participants will be enrolled in a single stage. 
Note for France only (Appendix 14.15): Participants with metastatic urothelial carcinoma should 
have previously received approved therapies, including platinum-based chemotherapy, an 
immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an antibody-drug conjugate.  Participants with metastatic 
urothelial carcinoma may also be enrolled if they are not candidates for any of the approved 
therapies.  Participants with other tumor types (Cohort 3) should either have received approved 
standard therapies for their tumor type or not be candidates for such therapies. 
Note for Spain only (Appendix 14.15): Participants with metastatic urothelial carcinoma should 
have previously received approved therapies, including platinum-based chemotherapy, an 
immune checkpoint inhibitor, and an antibody-drug conjugate.  Participants with metastatic 
urothelial carcinoma may also be enrolled if they are not candidates for any of the approved 
therapies or if they refuse these therapies.  Participants with other tumor types (Cohort 3) should 
either have received approved standard therapies for their tumor type, not be candidates for such 
therapies, or have refused these therapies.

4.2 
Study Stopping Criteria 
The sponsor may terminate the study at any time if the incidence and severity of AEs suggest 
that the risk-benefit to participants is no longer appropriate to continue the study.  Examples 
include, but are not limited to, cases of severe liver toxicity without an underlying cause or 
unexplained participant deaths not due to disease progression or extraneous causes. 
During termination of the study, the sponsor will ensure that the rights of the participants are 
fully protected.  See Section 11.7 for general study discontinuation procedures. 
4.3 
Study Duration 
All enrolled participants will receive TYRA-300 until disease progression, death, unacceptable 
toxicity, withdrawal of participant’s consent for treatment, withdrawal from the study at the 
discretion of the investigator, or study termination. 
The estimated duration of the study is 58 months. 
4.3.1 
End of Study 
End of Study is defined as the last participant’s last visit, up to and including the safety 
follow-up visit (may be conducted via telephone or video contact), if applicable.

5 STUDY POPULATION 
Participants must meet all inclusion criteria and none of the exclusion criteria at Screening to be 
enrolled in the study.  No deviations will be permitted from the inclusion or exclusion criteria.  
The investigator may contact the medical monitor to discuss eligibility of any given participant. 
Specific inclusion criteria are detailed in Section 5.1 and exclusion criteria are detailed in 
Section 5.2. 
5.1 
Inclusion Criteria 
5.1.1 
Phase 1, Part A 
1. Male and female participants who are 18 years of age or older on the day of signing the ICF. 
2. Life expectancy >12 weeks. 
3. Ability to understand and sign the ICF and comply with study procedures. 
4. ECOG PS ≤1. 
Note: Refer to Appendix 14.4 for ECOG PS. 
5. Participants with any histologically confirmed advanced solid tumor who have exhausted 
standard therapeutic options.  For doses of TYRA-300 above 120 mg daily, participants must 
have an eligible FGFR3 gene mutation or fusion (see Appendix 14.3.1 and 14.3.2) diagnosed 
by any authorized/approved or validated local test performed in a CLIA-certified (or regional 
equivalent) laboratory. 
6. Ability to swallow oral formulations. 
7. Disease evaluable by RECIST v1.1. 
8. Adequate organ and bone marrow function as demonstrated by the following: 
a. ANC ≥1500/mm3. 
b. Platelet count ≥75,000/mm3. 
c. International normalized ratio (INR) ≤1.5 × ULN. 
i. Participants treated with anticoagulants (eg, warfarin or heparin) will be allowed to 
participate provided no prior evidence of an underlying abnormality in these 
parameters exists.  Close monitoring of at least weekly evaluations will be performed 
until INR is stable based on a predose measurement as defined by the local standard 
of care. 
d. Total bilirubin ≤1.5 × ULN.  Documented Gilbert syndrome is allowed if total bilirubin is 
mildly elevated (<6 mg/dL). 

===PAGE 57===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 57 of 167 
e. ALT and AST ≤2.5 × ULN (≤3 × ULN for participants with liver involvement of their 
cancer). 
f. Serum albumin >2 g/dL.
