5.2 
Exclusion Criteria 
1. Participant received chemotherapy, targeted therapy, immunotherapy, or an investigational 
therapy within 4 weeks or 5 half-lives, whichever is shorter, before the first dose of study 
drug (within 6 weeks for nitrosoureas and mitomycin). 
2. Participant has not recovered from reversible toxicity of prior anticancer therapy (except 
toxicities that are not clinically significant including, but not limited to, alopecia, skin 
discoloration, or Grade 1 neuropathy). 
3. Had major surgery within 4 weeks prior to enrollment. 
4. Any reason that, in the view of the investigator, would substantially impair the ability of the 
participant to comply with study procedures and increase the risk to the participant.  
Examples include poorly controlled diabetes (glycated hemoglobin [HbA1c] >8%) and 
ongoing active infection requiring intravenous (IV) antibiotics. 
5. Females who are pregnant, breastfeeding, or planning to become pregnant within 180 days 
after the last dose of TYRA-300 and males who plan to father a child while enrolled in this 
study or within 180 days after the last dose of TYRA-300. 
6. Has impaired wound healing capacity defined as skin/decubitus ulcers, chronic leg ulcers, 
known gastric ulcers, or unhealed incisions. 
7. Has a serum phosphorus level >ULN during screening (within 14 days of treatment and prior 
to Cycle 1, Day 1) that remains >ULN despite medical management with phosphate binders. 
8. Any ocular condition likely to increase the risk of eye toxicity, including: 
a. History of or current evidence of central serous retinopathy (CSR; including ≥Grade 2 
CSR while receiving a prior FGFR inhibitor) or retinal vascular occlusion. 
b. Active wet, age-related macular degeneration. 
c. Diabetic retinopathy with macular edema. 
d. Uncontrolled glaucoma (per local standard of care). 
9. History of or current uncontrolled cardiovascular disease including: 
a. Unstable angina, myocardial infarction, or known congestive heart failure Class II to IV 
within the preceding 12 months. 
b. Cerebrovascular accident or transient ischemic attack within the preceding 3 months. 
c. Pulmonary embolism within the preceding 2 months. 
10. MRI of the brain must be performed at screening for all patients and, if untreated/newly 
diagnosed brain metastases are identified, the candidate is not eligible. 

===PAGE 63===

Protocol TYR300-101

a. The candidate may rescreen for the study after definitive treatment of brain metastases. 
b. Prior brain metastases treated at least 4 weeks prior to signing the full-study ICF or that 
are clinically and radiographically stable for at least 1 month prior to Cycle 1, Day 1 and 
do not require chronic corticosteroid treatment are allowed. 
11. Gastrointestinal disorders that will affect oral administration or absorption of TYRA-300. 
12. Known history of human immunodeficiency virus (HIV) infection, or active hepatitis B or C 
infection.

5.3 
Participant Withdrawal/Discontinuation Criteria 
5.3.1 
Discontinuation of Treatment 
A participant will be considered to have completed study treatment when he or she meets 1 of 
the treatment discontinuation criteria: progression of disease; death; discontinuation of therapy 
due to AEs or toxicity; withdrawal of the participant’s consent for continued participation in the 
study; or withdrawal from the study at the discretion of the investigator, including circumstances 
in which participant noncompliance with the protocol interferes with continued safe participation 
CCI
CCI
CCI

===PAGE 64===

Confidential 
Page 64 of 167 
in the study.  If a participant is discontinued at any time after enrollment into the study, the 
investigator will make every effort to follow the participant and complete the End of Treatment 
(EOT) assessments as well as the 30-day safety follow-up assessment, if the participant had 
initiated study drug, as shown in Section 7.1. 
If a patient withdraws consent during treatment, they are to be informed of the option that they 
can continue to attend follow up visits for the collection of study data. 
Participants will only be replaced during the dose escalation phase (Phase 1, Part A) if they do 
not receive at least 75% of the planned dose during the DLT evaluation period for reasons other 
than a TRAE, or discontinue the study for non-DLT reasons during the DLT evaluation period. 
Replacement of participants is not permitted in Phase 1, Part B and Phase 2. 
An electronic Case Report Form (eCRF) page should be completed for every participant who 
receives study drug.  The reason for any study treatment discontinuation should be indicated on 
this form.  The primary reason for a participant discontinuing from the study should be selected 
from the following standard categories: 
• Progression of Disease: Tumor growth as defined by RECIST v1.1. 
• Adverse Event (Adverse Reaction): Clinical or laboratory events occurred that, in the medical 
judgment of the investigator for the best interest of the participant, are grounds for 
discontinuation.  These events include SAEs and nonserious AEs regardless of relation to the 
study drug. 
• Death: The participant died. 
• Withdrawal of Consent: The participant desired to withdraw from further participation in the 
study in the absence of an investigator-determined medical need to withdraw.  If the 
participant gave a reason for withdrawing, the reason should be recorded in the eCRF.  If the 
participant actively withdraws consent for collection of safety follow-up data, no additional 
data will be collected. 
• Investigator’s Discretion: If the participant is removed from the study and has not met 
RECIST v1.1 criteria for disease progression, every reasonable effort should be made to 
continue to obtain computed tomography (CT) scans as outlined in the Schedule of 
Assessments (SoA) until PD is documented. 
• Termination of the Study by the Sponsor. 
• Lost to Follow-up: The participant stopped coming for visits and study personnel were 
unable to contact the participant.  Actions to be taken if a participant fails to return to the 
clinic for a required study visit are presented in Section 5.3.3. 
• Noncompliance.

• Other: The participant was discontinued for a reason other than those listed above.  The

5.3.2 
Participant Withdrawal from the Study 
A participant may withdraw from the study at any time at his/her own request, or may be 
withdrawn at any time at the discretion of the investigator for safety, behavioral, compliance, or 
administrative reasons. 
If a participant withdraws from the study, he/she may request destruction of any samples taken 
and not tested, and the investigator must document this in the site study records.  If the 
participant withdraws consent and withdraws from the study, the sponsor may retain and 
continue to use any data collected before such a withdrawal of consent. 
Study withdrawal may coincide with EOT.  See Table 7.1 and Table 7.2 for data and/or study 
procedures to be collected at the time of study withdrawal and follow‑up, and for any further 
evaluations that need to be completed. 
5.3.3 
Lost to Follow-up 
A participant will be considered lost to follow-up if he/she repeatedly fails to return for 
scheduled visits and is unable to be contacted by the study site. 
The following actions must be taken if a participant fails to return to the clinic for a required 
study visit: 
• The site must attempt to contact the participant and reschedule the missed visit as soon as 
possible and counsel the participant on the importance of maintaining the assigned visit 
schedule and ascertain whether or not the participant wishes to and/or should continue in the 
study. 
• Before a participant is deemed lost to follow-up, the investigator or designee must make 
every effort to regain contact with the participant; 3 attempts should be made (email, 
telephone/video call, and certified letter to the participant's last known mailing address or 
local equivalent methods).  These contact attempts should be documented in the participant's 
medical record.  After all attempts are exhausted, the participant will be considered to have 
withdrawn from the study.

6.1.3 
Handling and Storage 
Verbal and written instructions for proper storage, handling, and administration of the study drug 
will be given to the participant and will include instructions to contact the study site immediately 
if they experience problems with the study drug and/or administration. 
Site storage conditions should be monitored by the site personnel and reviewed by the monitor 
during site visits.  Deviations from the storage requirements must be documented and reported to 
the sponsor. 
Complete instructions for proper study drug storage and handling are provided in the Pharmacy 
Manual. 
6.2 
Shipment 
All study drugs will be supplied by the sponsor.  Capsules and tablets will be packaged in bottles 
(Section 6.1.1).  No study drug will be shipped to a site until written IRB/EC authorization has 
been received by the sponsor or its representative. 
6.3 
Treatment Assignment 
This is an open-label, non-randomized study so no blinding will be used.  All participants will 
receive an assigned dose of TYRA-300.  In Phase 1, intra-participant dose escalation may occur 
when protocol-specified criteria are met (Section 4.1). 
6.3.1 
Interactive Response Technology 
Each participant will be identified in the study by a participant identification (ID) number, which 
is a combination of the country, site ID, and participant number.  Site staff should contact the 
Interactive Response Technology (IRT) to obtain the participant ID number during Screening.  
Upon determining that the participant is eligible for study entry, the IRT will be contacted to 
obtain the treatment assignment.  Additionally, the IRT will be contacted on the day of each 
regular study visit to receive study drug assignment.  Additional details are provided in the IRT 
Manual. 
6.4 
Dose and Administration 
6.4.1 
Estimation of Safe Starting Dose 
6.4.1.1 
Phase 1, Part A – Dose Escalation (Advanced Solid Tumors) 
The initial dose for TYRA-300 will be 10 mg once daily, which is considered to be safe based on 
extrapolation from animal toxicological data and the anticipated safety profile based on known 
class effects of approved and investigational pan-FGFR inhibitors.  Refer to Section 1.3 for 
further information regarding starting dose rationale. 
Dose escalation will be initiated using an accelerated dose escalation design in which single 
participant cohorts for lower doses will be evaluated.  In the absence of a ≥Grade 2 TRAE during 

===PAGE 68===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 68 of 167 
the DLT evaluation period, DLs will increase step-wise up to 100% of the previous dose until a 
participant experiences a ≥Grade 2 TRAE during the DLT evaluation period, at which time the 
study will convert to an i3 + 3 schema.  Each subsequent dose escalation step will be determined 
based on the results with regard to safety, tolerability, and, if available, PK data.  Dose increases 
will not exceed 50% of the previous dose beyond DL3.  Intermediate doses as well as alternative 
dosing schedules may also be explored.  Details for the dose-escalating steps are given in 
Section 4.1. 
6.4.1.2 
Phase 2 –Expansion Cohorts in Selected Tumor Populations 
Participants in Phase 2 will receive TYRA-300 at the RP2D, selected using the totality of the 
Phase 1 data. 
6.4.1.3 
Duration of Treatment 
All enrolled participants will receive TYRA-300 daily until disease progression, death, 
unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of participant’s consent for treatment, or withdrawal from the 
study at the discretion of the investigator. 
6.4.1.4 
Administration Procedures 
• Participants should be instructed to take TYRA-300 at approximately the same time of day 
each day for once daily or twice daily dosing, with food or within 30 to 60 minutes after food 
or a small meal (eg, eggs, toast, yogurt, or small sandwich). 
• If vomiting occurs, the dose should not be repeated, and dosing should resume at the next 
dose.  Vomiting and dosing should be recorded in the clinical electronic data capture (EDC) 
system. 
• If the dose of TYRA-300 is missed within 12 hours (6 hours for patients on twice daily 
dosing) of the scheduled medication time, TYRA-300 should be taken as soon as possible the 
same day.  If the dose is missed by more than 12 hours (6 hours for patients on twice daily 
dosing) from the scheduled time, the dose should be skipped. 
• Overdose is an accidental or intentional use of TYRA-300 higher than the protocol assigned 
dose.  When an overdose occurs, the investigator should discuss with the sponsor's medical 
monitor and provide appropriate supportive care for drug overdose induced adverse 
reactions.  The investigator should also promptly report the adverse reactions to the sponsor. 
• Participants will be given reminder cards that provide information regarding when and how 
to take TYRA-300. 
Details of TYRA-300 dose units (ie, the number of capsules or tablets to be taken as a single 
dose) are provided in the Pharmacy Manual.

6.5 
Dose Reduction and/or Discontinuation Rules 
6.5.1 
Precautions 
Based on preclinical data, potential toxicities of TYRA-300 include hyperphosphatemia, 
hypercalcemia, liver function abnormalities, gastrointestinal abnormalities, and skeletal system 
disorders.  Based on clinical study results of pan-FGFR inhibitors, common toxicities may 
include hyperphosphatemia; hypercalcemia; nail, skin, and mucosal abnormalities; 
gastrointestinal abnormalities; ocular abnormalities (including CSR); fatigue; cough; and 
elevated lipase. 
Doses of TYRA-300 may be delayed or reduced in case of clinically significant hematologic and 
other toxicities that are possibly, probably, or definitely related to study drug therapy.  Toxicities 
will be graded using the CTCAE v5.0.  Intra-participant dose escalation can only occur in 
Phase 1, Parts A and B under the circumstances described in Section 4.1.  The dose of 
TYRA-300 can be reduced or delayed for an individual participant based on toxicities that are 
considered by the investigator to be related to study drug. 
6.5.2 
Dose Delays and Dose Modifications 
Treatment cycles are 28 days in duration, regardless of any dose interruptions. 
Treatment can be modified or terminated based on toxicity as described in Table 6.2.  For 
phosphorous, calcium, eye, liver, dry mouth/mucositis, skin, and nail toxicities, specific 
recommendations on the management guidelines are provided in Sections 6.5.3 through 6.5.8. 
Table 6.2: 
Toxicity Management 
Toxicity Grade 
TYRA-300 Action 
TYRA-300 Dose Modification 
1 
None 
Continue same dose 
2 
Consider dose interruption 
If interrupted, restart at the same dose or 1 DL lower, if 
necessary 
3 
Interrupt drug dose 
Restart TYRA-300 at 1 or 2 DLs lower once recovered to 
≤Grade 1 or baseline 
4 
Discontinue 
Discontinue 
5 
No action (fatal toxicity) 
No action (fatal toxicity) 
Abbreviations: DL = dose level. 
General toxicity management: 
• Participants with any grade of toxicity (Grade 1 to 4) should be provided supportive and 
symptomatic treatment, as appropriate. 
• Investigators in Phase 1 should follow the guidance in Table 6.3 for dose reduction 
recommendations.  Participants may have more than 1 dose reduction, and toxicity 
management may include dose interruption and dose reduction. 

===PAGE 70===

Confidential 
Page 70 of 167 
• If a participant experiences a DLT during Cycle 1, the next dose of TYRA-300 can be 
delayed up to 21 days for a Grade 3 non-hematologic DLT that lasts ≤7 days.  For Grade 4 
DLTs, and Grade 3 DLTs that last >7 days, participants must discontinue TYRA-300. 
• If TYRA-300 must be withheld >21 days for a study drug-related AE that fails to resolve to 
an acceptable level (eg, to ≤Grade 1 for a non-hematologic toxicity or to baseline), treatment 
with TYRA-300 should be discontinued unless the investigator and medical monitor agree 
that the participant is benefitting from continued therapy and can be safely restarted with 
appropriate dose reduction(s) and monitoring. 
• In all cases of clinically significant impaired wound healing, imminent surgery, or potential 
bleeding complications, it is recommended that TYRA-300 dose administration is 
interrupted, appropriate clinical laboratory data is carefully monitored, and supportive 
therapy is administered, where applicable.  Dose administration may be restarted when it is 
considered safe and at an appropriate dose, according to the investigator’s clinical judgment 
and in consultation with the study medical monitor.

For Grade 2 TRAEs, once the toxicity resolves to ≤Grade 1, the next dose of TYRA-300 may 
resume at the same DL or 1 DL below the current dose based on the severity of the AE and 
association with TYRA-300. 
If the toxicity fails to resolve to ≤Grade 1 within 21 days after the planned treatment date, the 
participant will be removed from the study except as noted above.  Participants who experience a 
Grade 4 non-hematological treatment-related toxicity will be removed from the study. 
For ≥Grade 3 AEs regarded as related to study drug, the next dose of TYRA-300 can be delayed 
for ≤21 days.  If the toxicity fails to resolve to ≤Grade 1 within 21 days after the scheduled 
dosing date, the participant may be discontinued from the study.

6.5.3 
Management of Hyperphosphatemia and Hypercalcemia 
During the study treatment period (including the DLT assessment period), all participants with 
reduced renal function (GFR <60 mL/min) may be counseled by the investigator and/or site staff 
to restrict daily phosphorous intake (see Appendix 14.10 for phosphorus-rich foods and lower 
phosphorous alternatives).  Guidelines for the clinical management of elevated serum 
phosphorous levels are presented in Table 6.4.  Laboratory assessments should be performed at 
least twice weekly to monitor serum phosphorous levels, if elevated above the ULN.

In cases of Grade 2 hypercalcemia concurrent with hyperphosphatemia or Grade 3 
hypercalcemia in the absence of hyperphosphatemia, TYRA-300 should be held and serum 
electrolytes monitored at least twice weekly until resolved to within the normal range.  
Participants can resume TYRA-300 with a dose reduction of 1 or 2 DLs at the discretion of the 
investigator and with agreement from the medical monitor.  Should Grade 2 hypercalcemia with 
hyperphosphatemia or Grade 3 hypercalcemia recur with dose reduction, TYRA-300 should be 
permanently discontinued. 
Note: If a participant has been deriving benefit from study drug and the investigator can 
demonstrate that reintroduction of TYRA-300 is in the best interest of the participant given the 
terminal nature of the disease, the study drug may be reintroduced at a lower dose and/or 
intensity if the medical monitor is in agreement with this assessment.  With appropriate 
reconsenting, the participant may resume TYRA-300 with a further dose reduction and 
monitoring of serum electrolytes at least twice weekly, along with appropriate clinical follow-up 
as designated by the investigator.  The investigator should also have the participant reconsent, 
explaining that reintroduction of investigational product could lead to increased risk of recurrent 
toxicities. 
6.5.4 
Management of Ocular Toxicity with Vision Changes 
FGFR targeted therapy is associated with ocular toxicities, including dry eye, CSR/retinal 
pigment epithelial detachments, blurred vision, and visual impairment.  Ophthalmological 
assessments are described in Section 8.2.6. 
If the participant has any ocular toxicity (such as dry eye, blurred vision, visual impairment, or 
flashing lights), it is recommended to promptly consult an ophthalmologist.  If the participant has 
any significant eye discomfort between scheduled visits during the treatment and safety 
follow-up periods, the participant should promptly contact the investigator for possible referral to 
an ophthalmologist. 
Guidelines for the management of eye toxicity are presented in Table 6.5.

6.5.5 
Management of Hepatotoxicity and Potential Drug-induced Liver Injury 
(Hy’s Law) 
Hepatotoxicity refers to ALT and/or AST and/or bilirubin increases and/or hepatic failure 
considered possibly related to TYRA-300.  Pan-FGFR inhibitors, particularly those that have 
potency against FGFR4, have been associated with hepatotoxicity in preclinical models and in 
clinical use.  In addition, TYRA-300 is an inhibitor of bile salt export pump (BSEP) in vitro. 
Participants with an AST/ALT value >3 × ULN with concomitant bilirubin >2 × ULN must be 
evaluated for the possibility of drug-induced liver injury (DILI), TYRA-300 should be held, and 
the event reported as an SAE.  A gastrointestinal specialist should be consulted.  As part of the 

===PAGE 76===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 76 of 167 
study safety review, other causes for the liver function test (LFT) abnormalities should be sought 
and excluded.  Assessments should include: 
• Concomitant medications (prescribed, over-the-counter, and recreational use). 
• Alcohol intake. 
• Hepatitis serology (hepatitis A, B, C, D, and E). 
• Liver ultrasound. 
• Hemolysis. 
• Eosinophilia. 
• Gilbert’s syndrome. 
• Possible liver biopsy. 
LFTs including AST, ALT, alkaline phosphatase, and total bilirubin should be monitored twice 
weekly to document resolution to ≤Grade 1 or baseline. 
If a specific cause other than DILI is identified and LFTs resolve to ≤Grade 1 or baseline, 
participants may restart TYRA-300 at 1 to 2 DLs levels lower after consultation with the medical 
monitor for assessment of benefit-risk.  LFTs should be monitored weekly for ≥4 weeks.  If 
Grade 2 AST and/or ALT recurs with total bilirubin >2 × ULN, TYRA-300 should be 
discontinued. 
Management guidelines for other instances of AST and/or ALT elevations are presented in 
Table 6.6.

Management of Dry Mouth and Stomatitis/Mucositis 
Guidelines for the clinical management of dry mouth (xerostomia) and stomatitis are provided in 
Table 6.7 and Table 6.8 respectively. 
General guidelines: 
• Good oral hygiene. 
• Use a soft toothbrush. 
• Use mild-flavored toothpaste. 
• Use saline-peroxide or salt and soda mouthwashes 3 to 4 times per day. 
• Use water-soluble lubrication agents such as artificial saliva (for dry mouth). 
• Avoid spicy, acidic, hard, and hot food and beverages.

6.5.7 
Management of Dry Skin and Skin Toxicity 
Guidelines for the clinical management of dry skin and skin toxicity are provided in Table 6.9. 
General guidelines: 
• Avoid unnecessary exposure to sunlight and excessive use of soap. 
• Avoid bathing in excess; use tepid rather than hot water. 
• Use moisturizers regularly; apply thick, alcohol-free and oil-in-water based emollient 
cream on exposed and dry areas of the body. 
• Avoid perfumed products, bubble bath, perfumed soaps, and take breaks from shaving. 
• Use broad spectrum sunscreen with a skin protection factor ≥15 and clothing that covers 
sun-exposed areas. 
• Wear cotton clothes next to skin rather than wool, synthetic fibers, or rough clothing. 
• Use occlusive alcohol-free emollient creams (jar or tub) for treatment of mild/moderate 
xerosis. 
• For scaly areas, use exfoliants (ammonium lactate 12% or lactic acid cream 12%).

Palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia syndrome (PPES): Management per local standard of care,

6.6 
Drug Accountability 
The investigator or designee must maintain an accurate record of the shipment and dispensing of 
study drug in a drug accountability log.  Drug accountability will be verified by the Clinical 
Research Associate during site visits and at the completion of the study. 
When drug accountability has been verified and a copy of the completed drug accountability log 
has been received by the drug depot or sponsor designee, the investigator will dispose of all used 
and unused study drug and packaging in accordance with the guidance in the Pharmacy Manual 
or study-specific and local regulations. 
6.7 
Treatment Compliance 
Study drug containers must be returned at each visit, as compliance will be assessed by capsule 
counts.  Noncompliance is defined as taking less than 80% or more than 120% of the assigned 
study drug dose during any outpatient evaluation period (visit to visit).  Discontinuation for 
noncompliance is at the investigator’s discretion and is to be noted on the eCRF (see 
Section 5.3). 
6.8 
Prior and Concomitant Medications 
All prescription and non-prescription medications (eg, over-the-counter drugs and herbal 
supplements) that participants report taking after completing the informed consent form and 
during the 28-day Screening period will be recorded in the eCRF as prior medications. 
All drugs and therapies, including over-the-counter medications and herbal supplements, used 
after the first dose of study drug should be recorded in the eCRF as concomitant medications. 
For each medication, documentation should list the trade or generic name, the total daily dose 
including units (or the dose, units, and scheduled and actual frequency of administration if the 
medication is not taken daily), the route of administration, and the reason for use. 
Changes, additions, or discontinuations to medications will be assessed and recorded in the 
eCRF during each study visit.  All as-needed prescriptions should be converted to reflect actual 
dose taken per day.

7 STUDY CONDUCT 
Unless otherwise indicated, all assessments will be performed by the investigator or designated 
study personnel. 
After signing the ICF, each participant will be screened to ensure eligibility for the study. 
A list of assessments to be performed at each scheduled visit is provided in Appendix 14.11.

The procedures to be performed throughout the study are outlined in the SoA for Phase 1 
(Parts A and B; Table 7.1) and for Phase 2 (Table 7.2). 
For unscheduled visits, blood sampling and other tests should be performed as clinically 
indicated. 
Procedures for instances where on-site visits are restricted due to the COVID-19 pandemic are 
presented in Appendix 14.9.2.

ECGs will be transmitted from the site electronically to the sponsor-designated central vendor for “collect and hold”.

Triplicate 12-lead ECGs will be performed with PK blood draws on C1D1 (predose, and 30 minutes and 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 hours postdose) and C1D15

the ECG measurements should be completed immediately (up to 10 minutes) before collection of the PK blood sample.  For BID cohorts, the first 
3 participants in a cohort will have a 12-hour triplicate ECG done immediately before the 12 hour PK blood draw on C1D1 and C1D15.

Hepatitis B virus and Hepatitis C virus testing required at Screening.

t

7.1.1 
Prescreening 
Prescreening with a validated local test performed by a CLIA-certified (or regional equivalent) 
laboratory  may be offered to potential candidates with metastatic urothelial carcinoma who have 
not had prior NGS testing.  For Phase 1, Part B, candidates with metastatic urothelial carcinoma 
who do not have tissue available for a validated local test, performed at a CLIA (or regional 
equivalent) laboratory, may undergo prescreening using a sponsor-designated central assay to 
determine eligibility.  For Phase 2, Cohorts 1 and 2, candidates with metastatic urothelial 
carcinoma may undergo a prescreening test using a sponsor-designated central assay prior to 
enrolling in the study (see Appendix 14.14 for prescreening eligibility).  Prescreening is optional 
and requires signing the prescreening ICF before any prescreening procedures can be performed. 
7.1.2 
Screening (Day -28 to -1) 
Screening is the interval between signing the ICF and the day the participant is enrolled in the 
study (Cycle 1, Day 1).  The screening visit is to occur within 28 calendar days prior to the start 
of study drug administration.  The assessments that are required to demonstrate eligibility may 
be performed over the course of 1 or more days.  
Any procedures conducted as part of the participants’ standard of care (eg, hematology, blood 
chemistry, imaging) and done prior to signing the ICF may be used for screening purposes 
provided the procedure meets the protocol-defined criteria and has been performed within 
28 days of Cycle 1, Day 1.  All information associated with eligibility requirements must be 
entered into the appropriate eCRF page. 
The following assessments and local laboratory samples required at Cycle 1, Day 1 may be 
performed within 2 days prior to Cycle 1, Day 1: 
• Concomitant medicine review. 
• COVID 19 signs, symptoms, and recent exposure (per local regulations). 
• Physical examination including weight. 
• Amsler grid screening. 
• Laboratory assessments (hematology, serum chemistry, and pregnancy testing). 
• Pharmacodynamic sampling (blood and urine). 
7.1.3 
Screen Failures 
A screen failure is defined as a participant who consents to participate in the study but does not 
meet the protocol-defined eligibility criteria.  Following a screen failure, a participant may be 
rescreened 1 time.  Participants who are rescreened must sign a new ICF and be assigned a new 
participant number. 

===PAGE 94===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 94 of 167 
The following screening assessments may be used if the rescreening is performed ≤28 days prior 
to Cycle 1, Day 1: 
• CT/magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scan performed ≤28 days prior to Cycle 1, Day 1. 
• Screening local laboratory assessments within the protocol-defined window. 
• Skin biopsy sample (applicable only for participants in Phase 1). 
• Archived tumor sample (if available). 
7.1.4 
End of Treatment 
For each participant, the treatment period will continue in 4-week cycles (ie, 28 days) until 
disease progression, unless the participant fulfills 1 of the discontinuation of treatment criteria.  
When the participant permanently discontinues study drug, the EOT visit should be performed. 
If the EOT visit coincides with a regular scheduled study visit, the EOT assessments will 
supersede those of the scheduled visit and the data should be entered in the EOT visit within the 
eCRF.  The participant should be encouraged to return for the safety follow-up visit. 
7.1.5 
Unscheduled Visits 
Unscheduled visits may be held at any time, at the investigator’s discretion, and appropriate 
clinical assessments and laboratory measurements will be performed based on AEs or other 
findings.  Data and results from any unscheduled visit should be recorded in the eCRF. 
If an unscheduled visit occurs due to AE, PK sample collection should be performed and sent to 
the central laboratory.  The timing of PK sample collection and date and time of last study drug 
administration should be recorded in the eCRF. 
7.1.6 
Safety Follow-up 
The safety follow-up period is the interval between the EOT visit and the scheduled follow-up 
visit, which should occur 28 to 35 days after the EOT visit (or after the last dose of study drug if 
the EOT visit was not performed).  AEs and SAEs must be collected up until at least 28 days 
after the last dose of study drug or the start of a new anticancer therapy.  Follow-up of ongoing 
AEs and SAEs must be collected for at least 90 days or until resolved to ≤Grade 1 unless, in the 
opinion of the investigator, the AE/SAE is unlikely to resolve. 
Reasonable efforts should be made to have the participant return for the safety follow-up visit 
and report any AEs that may occur during this period.  If the participant cannot return to the site 
for the safety follow-up visit, the visit may be conducted via telephone or video.  Sites should 
document this contact in the source. 
If a participant is scheduled to begin a new anticancer therapy before the end of the safety 
follow-up period, the safety follow-up visit should be performed before a new anticancer therapy 

===PAGE 95===

Protocol TYR300-101

Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 95 of 167 
is started.  Once a new anticancer therapy has been initiated, the participant will move into the 
survival follow-up period. 
7.1.7 
Survival Follow-up 
Once a participant has completed the safety follow-up visit and/or starts a new anticancer 
therapy, the participant will move into the survival follow-up period and should be contacted by 
telephone, video, email, certified mail, or visit at least every 12 weeks to assess for survival 
status until death, withdrawal of consent, or the end of the study, whichever occurs first.  
Survival status should be documented in the eCRF. 
For participants who do not intend to return to the study investigator for their ongoing care, 
follow-up should be maintained to assess for survival status until death, withdrawal of consent, 
or the end of the study, whichever occurs first.

8 DESCRIPTION OF ASSESSMENTS 
All assessments will be performed throughout the study as noted in the applicable SoA 
(Table 7.1 or Table 7.2). 
8.1 
Efficacy Assessments 
8.1.1 
Tumor Assessments 
Baseline tumor assessment will be completed within 28 days prior to the first dose of study drug.  
It is recommended to perform CT of the chest, abdomen, pelvic cavity, and any other location 
where disease is present at baseline; additional tumor imaging evaluation must be performed if 
clinically indicated or if suspicious lesions are identified in other areas.  A brain MRI is also 
required at Screening and as clinically indicated throughout the study.  The preferred tumor 
imaging assessment modality is CT with IV contrast; however, non-contrast CT of the chest and 
MRI of the abdomen and pelvis are permitted if IV contrast is clinically contraindicated.  
Baseline and postbaseline efficacy assessments will be performed using the same imaging 
method and, as far as possible, by the same investigator (RECIST v1.1, Appendix 14.12). 
Tumor imaging assessment should be performed every 8 weeks (±7 days) up to 48 weeks, and 
then every 12 weeks (±7 days) thereafter, regardless of study treatment status, until disease 
progression, death, unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of participant’s consent for treatment, or 
withdrawal from the study at the discretion of the investigator, whichever occurs first.  The 
timing of tumor imaging assessments will be calculated based on the date of the first dose of 
study drug (ie, Cycle 1, Day 1).  Participants who discontinue study drug for reasons other than 
disease progression or death should continue imaging assessments per the protocol-defined 
schedule until disease progression, death, withdrawal of participant’s consent for treatment, or 
withdrawal from the study at the discretion of the investigator.  If an imaging result shows CR or 
PR for the first time, it is necessary to repeat the tumor imaging to confirm the response; 
confirmatory tumor imaging must be performed at least 4 weeks after the prior imaging 
assessment.  Further details will be provided in an Imaging Manual. 
For participants enrolled in Phase 2, Cohorts 1 and 2 only, digital images of scans performed will 
be transmitted from the sites electronically to the sponsor-designated central radiology vendor 
for “collect and hold”. 
Efficacy assessments include ORR, DCR, DOR, TTR, and PFS (RECIST v1.1). 
8.2 
Safety Assessments 
Details of screening for COVID-19 symptoms and close contacts is presented in 
Appendix 14.9.1. 

===PAGE 97===

8.2.1 
Physical Examination and Height 
When required, a full physical examination will include examination of general appearance, skin, 
neck (including thyroid), eyes, ears, nose, throat, heart, lungs, abdomen, lymph nodes, 
extremities, and nervous system.  An AE form must be completed for all changes identified as 
clinically noteworthy.  Height without shoes will be recorded in centimeters.  At other time 
points as noted in the SoA, a brief or targeted exam that is focused on any changes since the 
previous exam may be conducted. 
For participants who are 18 to 21 years of age, serial height measurements will be recorded at 
screening, Cycle 1, Day 1, and every 8 weeks thereafter. 
8.2.2 
Vital Signs and Body Weight 
Vital signs will include body temperature (°C), respiratory rate, sitting radial pulse rate, and 
sitting systolic and diastolic blood pressures.  Sitting recordings are to be made after the 
participant has been sitting for at least 1 minute with their feet squarely on the floor and arms 
relaxed, bent at the elbow. 
Body weight without shoes will be recorded in kilograms. 
An AE form must be completed for all changes identified as clinically noteworthy. 
8.2.3 
Electrocardiogram 
12-Lead ECGs will be collected at the times specified in the schedule of activities and as 
clinically indicated (Table 7.1 and Table 7.2).  All scheduled ECGs should be performed after 
the participant has rested quietly for at least 10 minutes in a supine position.  Triplicate 12-lead 
ECG measurements will be obtained approximately 2 minutes apart.  When a triplicate 12-lead 
ECG measurement is scheduled at the time of a PK blood sample collection, the ECG 
measurements should be completed immediately (up to 10 minutes) before collection of the 
blood sample.  
For participants enrolled in Phase 1 (Parts A and B) only, ECGs will be transmitted from the site 
electronically to the sponsor-designated central vendor for “collect and hold”. 
The following variables should be measured and recorded in the eCRF, per the ECG printout: 
heart rate, RR, QT, PR, QRS intervals, and QTcF (calculated).  If QT prolongation is suspected, 
ECGs should be conducted in triplicate.  The Fredericia correction formula must be used and 
QTc reported as the average of each triplicate. 
8.2.4 
Clinical Laboratory Tests 
A certified laboratory local to the investigative site will perform all clinical laboratory 
assessments for safety (ie, serum chemistry, hematology assessments, and coagulation tests).  
The investigative site will enter the laboratory results and laboratory normal ranges into the 
eCRF.  Additional testing may be required by the sponsor based on emerging safety data.  
Additional tests may also be performed if clinically indicated.

Clinically significant abnormal laboratory findings are those that are not associated with the 
underlying disease, unless judged by the investigator to be more severe than expected for the 
participant’s condition. 
Screening laboratory assessments must be performed within 28 days prior to Cycle 1, Day 1.  If 
performed more than 28 days before Cycle 1, Day 1, then the tests must be repeated and 
eligibility confirmed before study drug administration on Cycle 1, Day 1.  If Screening 
laboratory assessments are performed within 7 days of Cycle 1, Day 1 and are found to be within 
the normal local laboratory limits, these results may be used in place of the local laboratory 
assessments scheduled on Cycle 1, Day 1. 
Laboratory sample collection on Cycle 1, Day 1 must be performed before study drug 
administration.  After Cycle 1, laboratory procedures can be conducted up to 3 days before or 
3 days after a planned study visit and results should be reviewed by the investigator or qualified 
designee and found to be in the acceptable range. 
The clinical laboratory tests listed in Table 8.1 are to be performed as indicated in the SoA.

8.2.5 
Adverse Events 
Any AE that occurs after the participant signs the ICF but before the first dose of TYRA-300 
will be recorded as medical history unless it is related to a study procedure.  All AEs occurring 
after the participant starts study treatment and up to 28 calendar days after the last dose of study 
treatment, or a new anti-cancer therapy has started, whichever comes first, will be recorded. 
See Section 9 for additional information. 
8.2.6 
Ophthalmological Assessments 
Ophthalmological examinations, including noncontact intraocular pressure, slit lamps, fundus 
examinations, and optical coherence tomography, should be performed regularly during the 
screening period, the treatment period, and the safety follow-up period.  Contact methods 
(Goldman tonometer, Tono-Pen, and Tono-pen Avia) and noncontact methods (air puff 
tonometry) may be used.  If possible, the same method for the measurement of intraocular 
pressure should be used at each assessment. 
Amsler grid screening will be conducted on all Cycle x, Day 1 visits where ophthalmology 
examinations are not performed (ie, Cycle 1, Day 1; Cycle 2, Day 1; Cycle 4, Day 1; Cycle 5, 
Day 1; Cycle 7, Day 1 Cycle 8, Day 1; etc).  Further details are provided in Appendix 14.13. 
In Phase 1, ocular examinations should be performed by an ophthalmologist.  For Phase 2, ocular 
examinations may be performed by an optometrist and any abnormal findings should be referred 
to, and confirmed by, an ophthalmologist. 
8.3 
Pharmacokinetic Assessments 
Plasma samples will be collected for the measurement of plasma concentrations of TYRA-300, 
as specified in Table 8.2.  Samples may be collected at additional timepoints during the study, if 
warranted and agreed upon between the investigator and the sponsor.  Samples collected for 
analyses of TYRA-300 plasma concentrations may also be used to evaluate safety or efficacy 
aspects related to concerns arising during or after the study.  The actual date and time (24-hour 
clock time) of each sample will be recorded.  The testing of PK samples will be performed by a 
sponsor-designated bioanalytical laboratory with a validated method.  
At the discretion of the sponsor, excess PK samples may be evaluated for TYRA-300 
metabolites.  These analyses will be performed by a bioanalytical contract research organization 
designated by the sponsor. 
Instructions for the collection and handling of biological samples will be provided in the 
Laboratory Manual.

8.5 
Circulating Tumor DNA Analyses 
Analyses such as DNA mutations, copy number variations, microsatellite instability, indels, 
and/or rearrangements in genes may be performed on ctDNA samples.  A pharmacogenomics 
sample is required at Screening as a genomic DNA control sample. 
Urine samples will be used as an exploratory source of cell-free DNA to identify cancer 
associated genes and genetic anomalies that are detected in archived tumor and ctDNA samples. 
Skin biopsy samples may be used as a source of matched somatic DNA controls to increase the 
accuracy/specificity of the mutation/alteration calls from blood and archived tumor samples.  
Individual germline variants will not be analyzed for this purpose.  Where required by local 
regulations, a separate consent will be used for DNA/RNA research. 
Details of the procedures to be followed for sample collection, storage, and shipment will be 
documented in a separate Laboratory Manual. 
8.6 
Skin Biopsy 
Skin biopsy will be performed in Phase 1 using 2 mm skin punch.  The skin sample will be used 
to assess gene and/or protein expression changes that are potential pharmacodynamic markers for 
FGFR3 inhibition.  The skin biopsy may also be used to help better understand skin toxicities 
associated with pan-FGFR inhibitors and the inhibition of other FGFR family members beyond 
FGFR3.  As noted in Section 8.5, residual skin samples may be used as a matched somatic 
control for mutational analysis.  Skin biopsies will not be performed in Phase 2. 
Skin tape strips may be used for bulk RNA expression analysis as well as the analysis of possible 
microbiome and cytokine changes that arise from skin Aes. 
8.7 
Archived Tumor Specimen 
An archived tumor specimen, if available, must be provided for participants in Phase 1, Part A 
with FGFR3 gene alterations only, all participants in Phase 1, Part B, and all participants in 
Phase 2 prior to Cycle 1, Day 1.  A new biopsy is not required if archived tumor tissue does not 
exist.  The most recent representative tumor tissue should be submitted. 
Archived tissue samples should be in the form of a paraffin-embedded tissue block (preferred) or 
at least 15 unstained formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded (FFPE) slides that are 4 to 5 µm thick, 
have at least 20% tumor content, and are accompanied by a copy of the pathology report.  For 
core needle biopsies, 3 to 4 cores should be aligned in the FFPE block such that cut slides will

Archived tumor specimen(s) should be shipped to the sponsor-designated central laboratory.

9 ADVERSE EVENT REPORTING 
9.1 
Definitions and Criteria 
9.1.1 
Adverse Events 
Per International Council for Harmonisation of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for 
Human Use (ICH) E2A,40 an AE is any untoward medical occurrence in a patient or clinical 
investigation subject administered a pharmaceutical product and which does not necessarily have 
to have a causal relationship with this treatment.  An AE can therefore be any unfavorable and 
unintended sign (including an abnormal laboratory finding, for example), symptom, or disease 
(new or exacerbated) temporally associated with the use of a medicinal product, whether or not 
considered related to the medicinal product. 
Medical interventions such as surgeries, diagnostic procedures, and therapeutic procedures are 
not AEs, but the action taken to treat the medical condition.  They should be recorded as 
treatment(s) of the AEs.   
All AEs should be reported promptly. Disease progression should not be reported as an AE. In 
addition, signs and symptoms that are considered to be directly related to the progression of 
disease (e.g. neurological symptoms caused by the progression of brain metastases) should not 
be recorded as AEs. 
9.1.2 
Serious Adverse Events 
An SAE or a serious adverse drug reaction (ADR) is any untoward event that at any dose meets 
any of the following criteria: 
• Death. 
• Life-threatening. 
• Inpatient hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization. 
• Persistent or significant disability/incapacity. 
• Congenital anomaly/birth defect in the offspring of a participant who received study drug. 
• Other: Important medical events that may not result in death, be life-threatening, or require 
hospitalization, may be considered an SAE when, based upon appropriate medical judgment, 
they may jeopardize the participant and may require medical or surgical intervention to 
prevent 1 of the outcomes listed in this definition.  Examples of such events are: 
o Intensive treatment in an emergency room or at home for allergic bronchospasm. 
o Blood dyscrasias or convulsions that do not result in inpatient hospitalization. 
o Development of drug dependency or drug abuse. 

===PAGE 106===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 106 of 167 
For deaths, the underlying or immediate cause of death should always be reported as an SAE; 
however, deaths due to progression of disease should not be reported as an SAE. In addition, 
signs and symptoms deemed to be directly related to the progression of disease should not be 
reported as an SAE. 
An AE is life-threatening if the participant was at immediate risk of death from the event; it does 
not refer to an event that hypothetically might have caused death if it were more serious.  For 
example, drug-induced hepatitis that resolved without evidence of hepatic failure would not be 
considered life-threatening even though drug-induced hepatitis can be fatal. 
All AEs requiring hospitalization should be considered SAEs.  Hospitalization for elective 
surgery or routine clinical procedures that are not the result of AEs (eg, elective surgery for a 
pre-existing condition that has not worsened) need not be considered AEs or SAEs.  If anything 
untoward is reported during the procedure, that occurrence must be reported as an AE, either 
‘serious’ or ‘nonserious’ according to the criteria outlined above. 
An AE is incapacitating or disabling if the experience results in a substantial and/or permanent 
disruption of the participant’s ability to carry out normal life functions. 
Any serious, untoward event that occurs subsequent to the reporting period that the investigator 
assesses as related to study drug should also be reported and managed as an SAE. 
9.1.3 
Unexpected Adverse Drug Reactions 
An unexpected ADR is a reaction for which the nature or severity is not consistent with the 
reference safety information in the IB.  Until this information is amended, expedited reporting is 
required for additional occurrences of the reaction.  Reports that add significant information on 
specificity or severity of a known, already documented SAE constitute unexpected events.  For 
example, an event more specific or more severe than described in the IB would be considered 
“unexpected”.  Specific examples would be (a) acute renal failure as a labeled ADR with a 
subsequent new report of interstitial nephritis, and (b) hepatitis with a first report of fulminant 
hepatitis. 
Guidance on reporting AEs and SAEs is described in Section 9.2. 
9.1.4 
Clinical Laboratory Changes 
Any abnormality in a laboratory value that is new in onset or which has worsened in severity or 
frequency from the baseline condition and meets 1 of the following criteria will be recorded on 
the AE pages of the eCRF: 
• Requires therapeutic intervention or diagnostic tests. 
• Leads to discontinuation of study drug. 
• Has accompanying or inducing symptoms or signs. 
• Is judged by the investigator as clinically significant. 

===PAGE 107===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 107 of 167 
Combined elevations of aminotransferases and bilirubin, either serious or nonserious, and 
whether or not causally related, meeting the criteria of a potential Hy’s Law case (total bilirubin 
level ≥ 2 × ULN with simultaneously ALT or AST ≥ 3 × ULN) should always be reported to the 
sponsor as soon as possible following the procedures outlined in Section 9.2.2 for SAE reporting, 
with the investigator’s assessment of seriousness, causality, and a detailed narrative. 
9.1.5 
Adverse Events of Special Interest 
An adverse event of special interest (AESI) is an AE (serious or nonserious) of scientific and 
medical concern specific to study drug, for which ongoing monitoring and, if meeting the SAE 
criteria, immediate notification by the investigator to the sponsor is required.  Such AESIs may 
require further investigation to characterize and understand them.  The AESIs for this study 
include: 
• Hyperphosphatemia (elevated serum phopshorous, blood phosphorous elevated, etc). 
• CSR (blurred vision, retinal detachment, loss of vision, etc). 
• Stomatitis (dry mouth, mucositis, dysguesia, mouth sores, etc). 
• PPES (hand-foot syndrome, skin rash, dry skin, calciphylaxis, skin ulcers, etc). 
• Onycholysis (nail loss, nail bed infection, etc). 
• Alopecia (hair loss, baldness, etc). 
• Hepatotoxicity ≥Grade 3 (AST, ALT, total bilirubin elevations). 
The reporting of AESIs should follow the procedures for reporting AEs outlined in Section 9.2.1.  
AESIs should be identified on the AE eCRF.  
9.1.6 
Assessment of Severity 
CTCAE v5.0 will be used to assess AEs.  Each AE will be classified according to the criteria 
outlined in Table 9.1.41

Grade 
Severity 
Description 
1 
Mild 
Asymptomatic or mild symptoms; clinical or diagnostic 
observations only; intervention not indicated. 
2 
Moderate 
Minimal, local or noninvasive intervention indicated; 
limiting age-appropriate instrumental ADL.a

a 
Instrumental ADL refers to preparing meals, shopping for groceries or clothes, using the telephone,

Abbreviations: ADL = activities of daily living, AE = adverse event. 
Severity versus seriousness: Severity is used to describe the intensity of a specific event while 
the event itself may be of relatively minor medical significance (such as severe headache).  
Severity is not the same as “seriousness”, which is based on participant/event outcome at the 
time of the event. 
When changes in the severity of an AE occur more frequently than once a day, the maximum 
severity for the experience should be noted.  If the severity category changes over several days, 
those changes should be recorded as separate AEs (with distinct onset dates). 
9.1.7 
Assessment of Relationship 
Each AE will be assessed as to its relationship to the study drug, based on the following criteria.  
Although the attribution by the investigator will be collected for reported events, for analytic 
purposes a temporal association with the use of the study drug will be assumed sufficient for at 
least plausible association. 
Not related: 
No causal relationship exists between the study drug and the AE, but 
an obvious alternative cause exists, eg, the participant’s underlying 
medical condition or concomitant therapy. 
Possibly related: 
A connection with the administration of the study drug appears 
unlikely, but cannot be ruled out with certainty.  An AE may be 
considered possibly related if or when it meets 2 of the following 
criteria: (1) it follows a reasonable temporal sequence from 
administration of the study drug; (2) it could not readily have been 
produced by the participant’s clinical state, environmental or toxic 
factors, or other modes of therapy administered to the participant; or 
(3) it follows a known pattern of response to the study drug. 
Related: 
There is a reasonable/plausible possibility that the AE may have been 
caused by the study drug. 

===PAGE 109===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 109 of 167 
When assessing the relationship to the study drug, the following criteria will be considered: 
• Positive rechallenge. 
• Positive dechallenge (resolution upon stopping the suspect study drug, in absence of other 
intervention or treatment). 
• Known class effect. 
• Biological plausibility. 
• Lack of alternative explanation (eg, a concomitant drug or disease). 
9.1.8 
Action Taken Regarding Study Drug 
The action taken regarding the study drug as a result of an AE should be selected from 1 of the 
categories listed below and recorded on the eCRF:  
• Dose Not Changed: No change in study drug dose was made. 
• Dose Reduced: The dose of study drug was reduced. 
• Drug Interrupted: The study drug was temporarily stopped. 
• Drug Withdrawn: The study drug was permanently stopped. 
• Not Applicable: Participant died, study drug had been completed prior to reaction/event, or 
reaction/event occurred prior to the start of treatment. 
• Unknown. 
9.1.9 
Other Action Taken for Event 
Other action(s) taken as a result of the AE should be selected from 1 of the categories listed 
below and recorded on the eCRF: 
• None (ie, no treatment was required). 
• Therapy(ies) required (ie, prescription and/or over-the-counter medication was required to 
treat the AE). 
• Hospitalization or prolongation of hospitalization required (ie, hospitalization was required 
or prolonged because of the AE, whether medication was required). 
• Other.

9.1.10 
Adverse Event Outcome 
The outcome of the AE should be selected from 1 of the categories listed below and recorded on 
the eCRF: 
• Recovered/Resolved (ie, the participant fully recovered from the AE with no residual effect 
observed). 
• Recovering/Resolving (ie, the AE improved but has not fully resolved). 
• Not Recovered/Not Resolved (ie, the AE is still present and observable). 
• Recovered/Resolved with Sequelae (ie, the residual effects of the AE are still present and 
observable, including sequelae/residual effects). 
• Fatal (ie, ‘fatal’ should be used when death is a direct outcome of the AE). 
• Unknown. 
9.2 
Reporting Procedures and Requirements 
9.2.1 
Adverse Events 
Each time the participant is seen or contacted by the investigator or the study staff, be it at the 
study clinic, hospital, or over the telephone, the investigator will determine whether any AE has 
occurred by evaluating the participant.  AEs may be directly observed, reported spontaneously 
by the participant, or by questioning the participant at each contact.  Participants should be 
questioned in a general way, without asking about the occurrence of any specific symptoms.  If 
AEs occur, the first concern will be the safety of the study participants.  All AEs will be followed 
until resolved or stable and the outcome documented in the participant’s source 
documentation/medical record, and on the eCRF. 
Any AE that occurs after the participant signs the ICF but before the first dose of TYRA-300 
will be recorded as medical history unless it is related to a study procedure.  All AEs occurring 
after the participant starts study treatment and up to 28 calendar days after the last dose of study 
treatment, or a new anti-cancer therapy has started, whichever comes first, will be recorded in the 
participant’s source documentation/medical record and on the AE page of the eCRF.  If the 
investigator detects an AE in a participant after the last scheduled study visit and considers the 
event possibly related or related to prior study treatment, the investigator should report it to the 
sponsor or designated representative. 
For each reported AE, the following are to be reported: 
• Description of the AE: 
o Whenever possible, an AE or SAE will be reported using a diagnostic term, 
(eg, “common cold” or “upper respiratory infection” rather than “runny nose, cough, mild 
fever”). 

===PAGE 111===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 111 of 167 
o When a diagnosis is unavailable, report the primary sign or symptom as the AE or SAE 
term with additional details included in the narrative until the diagnosis becomes 
available. 
o If the signs and symptoms are distinct and do not suggest a common diagnosis, report 
them as individual entries of AEs or SAEs. 
• Start and end date and times of the AE. 
• Whether the AE is an SAE or AESI. 
• Severity (Section 9.1.6). 
• Relationship to the study drug (Section 9.1.7). 
• Action taken regarding the study drug (Section 9.1.8). 
• Other action taken for the AE (Section 9.1.9). 
• Outcome (Section 9.1.10). 
Illnesses that are present before the start of TYRA-300 administration are to be regarded as 
concomitant illnesses and recorded in the medical history.  Investigators should document all 
significant illnesses that the participant has experienced within 28 days of Screening as a prior 
illness.  Illnesses first occurring or detected during the study and/or worsening of a concomitant 
illness during the study are to be documented as AEs. 
All clinical laboratory results, vital signs, and ECG results or findings should be evaluated by the 
investigator to determine their clinical significance.  Isolated abnormal clinical laboratory test 
results, vital sign findings, or ECG findings (ie, not part of a reported diagnosis) should be 
reported as AEs if they are symptomatic, lead to study drug discontinuation, dose reduction, 
require corrective treatment, or constitute an AE in the investigator’s clinical judgment. 
All AEs and/or laboratory abnormalities identified as critical to safety evaluations should be 
reported to the sponsor.  If an AE requires an Urgent Safety Measure (USM) (ie, an action 
needed to be taken in order to eliminate hazard to a participant’s health or safety), the Principal 
Investigator (PI) or designated representative must report the USM instigated by the site to the 
sponsor within 24 hours of becoming aware of the event.  A USM can be instigated by either the 
investigator or the sponsor and can be implemented before seeking approal from Human 
Research Ethics Committees or institutions. 
9.2.2 
Serious Adverse Events 
Each AE will be assessed to determine whether it meets seriousness criteria (Section 9.1.2).  If 
the AE is considered serious, the investigator should report this event to 
 
pharmacovigilance and to the IRB/EC according to its standard operating procedures (SOPs).

reports from other health care professionals, discharge summaries, or other information that has 
been gathered about the event shall be transmitted to the sponsor and maintained in the 
participant’s source documentation/medical record.

9.2.3 
Pregnancy During the Study 
If a participant, or the female partner of a male participant, becomes pregnant during the study, 
the investigator will notify the sponsor or designated representative immediately after the 
pregnancy is confirmed.  The investigator will report any pregnancies using the eCRF Pregnancy 
Reporting Form within 24 hours of becoming aware of the event.  The investigator will also 
(1) notify the participant’s physician that the participant was being treated with TYRA-300, and 
(2) follow the progress of the pregnancy and document the outcome of the pregnancy.  
Pregnancy outcome information should be forwarded to the sponsor or designated representative 
when available. 
Participants who become pregnant during the study will not be eligible for any further study 
treatments.  The investigator and Tyra Biosciences, Inc. will determine, on a case-by-case basis 
and predicated on termination of the pregnancy, whether the participant will continue in the 
study and, if so, which study measures will be collected. 
The investigator should make every effort to follow the participant until completion of the 
pregnancy and complete the exposure in utero (EIU) Reporting Form eCRF with complete 
pregnancy outcome information, including normal delivery and induced abortion.  Any adverse 
pregnancy outcome, either serious or nonserious, should be reported in accordance with study 
procedures.  If the outcome of the pregnancy meets the criteria for immediate classification as an 
SAE (ie, post-partum complications, spontaneous or induced abortion, stillbirth, neonatal death, 
or congenital anomaly, including that of an aborted fetus), the investigator should follow the 
procedures for reporting SAEs outlined in Section 9.2.2.  The PI or designated representative 
must report to the sponsor within 24 hours of becoming aware of any occurrences of congenital 
anomaly/birth defect arising from any pregnancy of a participant or partner.  In the event the 
eCRF system is unavailable, a back-up paper Pregnancy Reporting Form will be available for 
site staff to complete following the reporting guidelines as outlined in Section 9.2.2. 
For reports of pregnancy in the female partner of a male participant, the eCRF Pregnancy Report 
Form and EIU Reporting form (or SAE form if applicable) should be completed with the 
participant’s ID number and year of birth, and details regarding the female partner should be 
entered in the narrative section.

10 STATISTICS 
10.1 
General Procedures 
The statistical analysis will be undertaken by 
 in collaboration with Tyra 
Biosciences, Inc. 
A detailed Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) will be finalized and signed before database lock and 
before analysis of the study being carried out.  Any deviations from the analyses described below 
will be included in the SAP, which will be included in the clinical study report. 
Data will be reported separately for participants in Phase 1 and Phase 2.  Data will be 
summarized and tabulated using the latest available version of SAS® statistical analysis software.  
Descriptive statistics will be utilized for all safety, efficacy, and PK/pharmacodynamic 
parameters where appropriate.  Data will be summarized using descriptive statistics (number of 
participants, mean, median, standard deviation, minimum, maximum) for continuous variables 
and using frequencies and percentages for discrete variables.  Data will be summarized by phase, 
dose, part, and cohort unless otherwise specified.  Time-to-event variables will be summarized 
using Kaplan Meier methods and figures for the estimated median time.  All data collected will 
be presented in participant listings.  Safety and tumor response data will be compared over time 
to assess change from baseline during treatment (baseline is defined as the screening tumor 
assessment for tumor response).  DL differences will be assessed using appropriate parametric or 
non-parametric significance tests, as applicable. 
PK data will be analyzed using PhoenixTM WinNonlin® (Version 8.1 or higher).  PK and 
pharmacodynamic parameters will be summarized using descriptive statistics.  If data warrant, 
the relationship between PK and pharmacodynamic parameters will be explored.  
 
 
 
. 
No estimands were defined for this non-randomized study. 
10.2 
 Sample Size 
For Phase 1, the total number of participants enrolled will depend on the number of escalation 
cohorts and the number of dose expansion cohorts evaluated.  It is estimated that up to 
80 participants could be enrolled in Parts A and B combined (up to 30 participants in Part A and 
up to 50 participants in Part B). 
For Phase 2, approximately 110 participants will be enrolled in each of Cohorts 1 and 2.  A 
Simon’s 2-stage design will be used for enrollment.  In the first stage, 39 participants will be 
accrued and if there are 6 or fewer responses, enrollment to the cohort will be stopped for 
futility.  Otherwise, 71 additional participants will be accrued for a total of 110 participants.  The 
null hypothesis will be rejected if 23 or more responses are observed in 110 participants.  This 
design yields a 1-sided type I error rate of 5% and a power of 80% when the true response rate is 
25%.

CCI
CCI

Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 115 of 167 
Cohort 3 is exploratory and approximately 40 participants will be enrolled in a single stage. 
10.3 
Analysis Populations 
10.3.1 
Safety Analysis Set 
The Safety Analysis Set (SAS) will include all participants who receive at least 1 dose of 
TYRA-300.  All safety analyses will be based on the SAS. 
10.3.2 
Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Sets  
The Phase 1 Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set (EAS1) will include all participants enrolled in 
Phase 1 who receive at least 1 dose of TYRA-300 and have a baseline disease (imaging) 
assessment. 
For sensitivity analysis, a second Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set for Phase 1 (EAS1a) will 
include all participants who received at least one dose of TYRA-300, have a baseline disease 
(imaging) assessment, and have an eligible FGFR3 alteration by dose level. 
The Phase 2 Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set (EAS2) will include all participants enrolled in 
Phase 2, Cohorts 1 or 2 who have FGFR3 gene alterations confirmed by a sponsor-designated 
central assay, received at least 1 dose of TYRA-300, and have a baseline disease (imaging) 
assessment. 
For sensitivity analysis, a second Efficacy-evaluable Analysis Set for Phase 2 (EAS2a) will 
include all participants enrolled in Cohorts 1 or 2, regardless of whether FGFR3 gene alterations 
are confirmed by a sponsor-designated central assay, who received at least 1 dose of TYRA-300, 
and have a baseline disease (imaging) assessment. 
The EAS1, EAS1a, EAS2, and EAS2a will be used for response analysis and efficacy related 
data. 
10.3.3 
Dose-limiting Toxicity-evaluable Analysis Set (Phase 1 Only) 
The DLT-evaluable Analysis Set (Phase 1 Only) will include all participants enrolled in Phase 1 
for dose escalation purposes who experience a DLT within the DLT evaluation period or those 
without a DLT, but who complete safety assessments through the DLT evaluation period and 
receive at least 75% of the planned total dose during that time.  Non-DLT-evaluable participants 
will be replaced.  This analysis set will be used for the analysis of MTD. 
10.3.4 
PK Analysis Set 
The PK Analysis Set will include all participants who receive at least 1 dose of study drug and 
have at least 1 postdose PK blood sample collected.  Additional participants may be excluded 
from the PK Analysis Set at the discretion of the pharmacokineticist.  All PK analyses will be 
based on this analysis set.

Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101

10.4 
Statistical Methods 
10.4.1 
Determination of Maximum Tolerated Dose and Recommended Phase 2 Dose 
The criteria for MTD will follow the i3 + 3 design with a target toxicity probability of 0.3 and 
equivalence interval of 0.25 to 0.35. 
The determination of RP2D will be made by the study’s SRC after taking into account the MTD 
and maximum administered dose, as well as other factors. 
10.4.2 
Protocol Deviations 
All protocol deviations will be assessed and documented on a case-by-case basis before database 
lock.  Protocol deviations considered to have a serious impact on the efficacy results will lead to 
the relevant participant(s) being excluded from the PK Analysis Set.  Protocol deviations should 
be collected by site and grouped into different categories, such as: 
• Those who entered the study even though they did not satisfy the entry criteria. 
• Those who developed withdrawal criteria during the study but were not withdrawn. 
• Those who received the wrong treatment or incorrect dose. 
• Those who received an excluded concomitant treatment. 
10.4.3 
Participant Disposition 
The number of participants who entered the study and the number of participants present at each 
scheduled visit will be summarized.  Recruitment will be summarized by country and site.  The 
reason and timing for withdrawal will be summarized. 
Details for the reasons and number of the screening failures will be tabulated. 
10.4.4 
Demographic and Baseline Characteristics 
Participant demographics and baseline characteristics will be summarized using descriptive 
statistics. 
10.4.5 
Analysis of Efficacy 
Response assessment data, duration of objective response (for responders), and PFS will be 
summarized and listed for all participants with measurable disease. 
Descriptive summaries will be provided for the best overall response (BOR). 
ORR is defined as the proportion of participants with CR or PR as determined by the investigator 
using RECIST V1.1.  Participants with missing or no response assessments will be classified as 
non-responders.  The point estimates of the ORR and the 2 sided 95% exact CI will be presented 
by dose for Phase 1 and by cohort for Phase 2. 

===PAGE 117===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 117 of 167 
DCR is defined as the proportion of participants having a CR, PR, or SD for >12 weeks.  The 
point estimates of the DCR and the 2-sided 95% exact CI will be presented by dose and cohort. 
Among participant with an objective response, DOR will be defined as the time from the initial 
CR or PR to the time of relapse or death, whichever occurs first.  For participants who do not die 
or experience disease progression before the end of the study or who are lost to follow-up, DOR 
will be censored at the day of last tumor assessment. 
PFS is defined as the time from the date of first study drug administration to the earliest date of 
documented disease progression or death. 
PFS and OS will be listed for Phase 1, and Kaplan Meier survival curves will be used to present 
the DOR, PFS, and OS time by cohort in Phase 2. 
Waterfall plots will be used to depict graphically the maximum decrease from baseline in the 
sum of the diameters of target lesion.  Swimmer plot will be used to show the occurrence of 
clinical outcomes of interest over time (eg, TTR, DOR, disease progression, treatment 
discontinuation, and death). 
Details of the analyses, including analyses of the secondary and exploratory endpoints, will be 
provided in the SAP. 
10.4.6 
Analysis of Safety 
10.4.6.1 
Extent of Exposure 
Extent of exposure will be described by whether the participant took any study drug along with 
the number of days of exposure (last date of dosing minus first day of dosing plus 1).  No 
allowance will be made for breaks in therapy. 
10.4.6.2 
Adverse Events 
AEs will be coded by primary system organ class and preferred term using the Medical 
Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA).  The number and percentage of participants 
reporting treatment emergent AEs will be tabulated for the SAS by MedDRA preferred term and 
system organ class with a breakdown by relationship to study drug and by severity.  If there are 
any AEs leading to the discontinuation of the study or death, separate data listings will be 
generated. 
A DLT is defined as any of the specified TRAEs (refer to Section 4.1), occurring during the DLT 
evaluation period.  All DLTs will be reported during the study.  The observed DLT rate in each 
dose cohort will be calculated by the crude proportion of participants who experience DLT using 
the DLT-evaluable Analysis Set.  Multiple concurrent AEs leading to a DLT will be considered a 
single DLT.  The estimate of the DLT rate will be accompanied by a 2-sided 95% exact binomial 
CI. 

===PAGE 118===
Tyra Biosciences, Inc. 
Protocol TYR300-101
TYRA-300 
Version 4.0, 06 Dec 2023
 
Confidential 
Page 118 of 167 
10.4.6.3 
Electrocardiogram 
The 12-lead ECG data will be listed for each participant and timepoint.  Observed values as well 
as change from baseline will be summarized descriptively.  Clinically significant worsening from 
baseline or new clinically significant ECG abnormalities that were considered by the investigator 
to be AEs will be presented in the AE analyses. 
10.4.6.4 
Vital Signs 
Vital signs data will be presented for each participant and timepoint.  Clinically significant 
worsening from baseline or new clinically significant vital sign finding that were considered by 
the investigator to be AEs will be presented in the AE analyses.  Observed values as well as 
change from baseline data will be summarized descriptively. 
10.4.6.5 
Physical Examination 
Physical examination findings will be listed.  Clinically significant worsening from baseline or 
new clinically significant physical examination abnormalities that were considered by the 
investigator to be AEs will be presented in the AE analyses. 
10.4.6.6 
Clinical Laboratory Tests 
Laboratory results will be presented for each participant.  Flags will be attached to values outside 
of the laboratory’s reference limits.  Clinically significant worsening from baseline or new 
clinically significant laboratory test abnormalities that were considered by the investigator to be 
AEs will be presented in the AE analyses.  Clinical laboratory tests (observed values and change 
from baseline) will be summarized descriptively. 
A summary table of abnormal values of safety laboratory tests will also be presented. 
10.4.6.7 
Other Variables Related to Safety 
ECOG PS (Appendix 14.4) will be summarized and listed for each participant and timepoint. 
10.4.7 
Pharmacokinetic and Pharmacodynamic Analysis 
TYRA-300 levels will be determined using blood samples collected before and after dosing.  
These determinations will be used to calculate the PK profiles for each evaluable participant at 
each DL administered.  PK parameters will include, but are not limited to, accumulation ratio, 
Cmax, Tmax, AUC0-last, AUCTau, AUC0-∞(after first dose only), Vd/F, CL/F, and t1/2, where 
possible. 
The PK parameters will be listed and summarized in tabular formats using descriptive statistics.  
TYRA-300 concentrations will be plotted against time points (actual time points for individual 
participants and nominal time points for the mean concentrations).  Individual and summary PK 
parameters will be listed and summarized in tabular format using descriptive statistics (n, mean, 
standard deviation, coefficient of variation [CV], median, minimum, maximum, geometric mean, 
geometric CV).

Population PK-based modeling approach may also be applied for PK characterization and PK 
covariate analyses.  The steady-state PK profile may be projected based on the population PK 
model. 
 
  Changes from 
baseline in pharmacodynamic parameters will be explored as appropriate.  The relationship 
between TYRA-300 concentrations and selected efficacy and safety outcomes may be explored.  
  Exploratory PK 
and pharmacodynamic analyses will be conducted as appropriate. 
10.4.8 
Interim Analysis and Monitoring 
A formal interim analysis is not planned for this study; however, the safety and efficacy results 
of the study will be monitored to justify escalation to each dose cohort in Phase 1 by the SRC.  In 
Phase 2 of the study, a Data and Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) will review reports of interim 
safety and efficacy data (refer to Section 11.3).

11 STUDY MANAGEMENT AND RESPONSIBILITIES 
11.1 
Ethics 
11.1.1 
Good Clinical Practice 
The study will be performed in accordance with this protocol, local national laws (as applicable), 
ICH guideline for GCP,42 and the most recent guidelines of the Declaration of Helsinki.43 
11.1.2 
Institutional Review Board/Independent Ethics Committee 
Conduct of the study must be approved by an appropriately constituted IRB or EC.  Approval is 
required for the study protocol, IB, protocol amendments, ICFs, participant information sheets, 
and advertising materials.  No study drug will be shipped to a site until written IRB/EC 
authorization has been received by the sponsor or its representative. 
11.1.3 
Informed Consent 
For each study participant, a written ICF or, in countries where permitted and approved, an 
electronic consent will be obtained before any protocol-related activities are performed.  As part 
of this procedure, the investigator or a designated representative must explain orally and in 
writing the nature, duration, and purpose of the study, and the action of the study drug in such a 
manner that the participant and (if applicable) appointed guardian are aware of the potential 
risks, inconveniences, or adverse effects that may occur.  Participants should be informed that 
they may withdraw from the study at any time.  They will receive all information that is required 
by local regulations and ICH guidelines.  The PI or a designated representative will provide the 
sponsor or its representative with a copy of the IRB/EC approved ICF before the start of the 
study. 
11.1.4 
Confidentiality 
All information generated in this study is considered highly confidential and must not be 
disclosed to any person or entity not directly involved with the study unless prior written consent 
is gained from the sponsor.  However, authorized regulatory officials, IRB/EC personnel, the 
sponsor and its authorized representatives are allowed full access to the records. 
11.2 
Auditing and Monitoring 
To ensure accurate, complete, and reliable data, 
 will provide instructional 
material to the study sites and the sponsor at a start-up training session to instruct the 
investigators and study coordinators.  This session will give instruction on the protocol, the 
completion of the eCRFs, and study procedures.  
This study will be monitored for quality assurance at all stages of its development by the clinical 
research personnel employed by the sponsor or its representative.  A Monitoring Plan will be 
developed and approved by the sponsor and 
 using a risk-based monitoring 
approach with the primary focus on critical data and processes that if inaccurate, not performed, 
or performed incorrectly, would threaten the protection of human participants or the integrity of

CCI

Confidential
